The colour field cannot place an island of this size even as an oracle (M149 needs 36 tiles for 0.80 and ours average 6.7); the seams between islands are too sparse (0.99 contacts per island, M155); the content prior is at chance; and no island ever spans the board's 24 columns, which is the only mechanism that would localise one without external evidence -- the widest reaches 15.3 columns and is 32% correct. Islands are recoverable and unplaceable
M162 | A larger, higher-resolution coarse field | REJECTED | n=12, ch=64, dim=192, hidden=768 against the n=8, ch=48 baseline; same data and schedule | 12x12 model at steps 4000/5000/6000/7000: +0.0092/+0.0104/+0.0096/+0.0112. The 8x8 baseline finished at +0.0147 | capacity and output resolution are not what limits the field. M138 put the 12x12 ceiling at +0.084 and we reach an eighth of it, so the shortfall is information, not architecture -- a bag of tiles genuinely does not say where they go, and the only part it does say is the part the blind twin already captures
M163 | Consensus across the whole model roster | KEY NEGATIVE / AN INFORMATION CEILING | 8 held-out boards; seven independent opinions -- seam_embed_v1, v2 and mix, two much smaller ch=64 networks from the invariance arms, the joint pair head, and MGC, which is not learned at all. An edge scores one vote per model that calls it mutual top-1; edges are then ranked by votes and, within a vote count, by the best margin any voter gave it | precision by votes required: 1 -> 1870 edges at 0.212, 2 -> 899 at 0.382, 3 -> 619 at 0.500, 4 -> 465 at 0.595, 5 -> 298 at 0.696, 6 -> 205 at 0.794, 7 -> 19 at 0.892. Islands from the top-K ranked edges: K=100 gives trusted coverage 0.158 at internal accuracy 0.900, K=150 gives 0.159 at 0.856, K=200 gives 0.120 at 0.806, K=400 gives 0.023 | consensus behaves exactly as an ensemble should at the EDGE level -- precision rises monotonically with agreement, from 0.21 at one vote to 0.89 at seven -- and it changes nothing downstream. Three unrelated selection schemes now converge on the same number: loop closure 0.154, two-model agreement plus margin 0.158, seven-model vote plus margin 0.159. That coincidence is the result. The limit on trusted coverage is not the selection rule, it is how much consistent adjacency information the tiles contain, and about 15-16% of the board is all of it. Consistent with M141: the corruption leaves a tile's own identity intact (0.66 among 576) while adjacency stays weak, because neighbouring tiles share no pixels at all -- only the image's continuity across the seam
M164 | The joint scorer with an UNFROZEN trunk | REJECTED / LAST LEVER SPENT | train_seam_joint.py --unfreeze, 6000 steps at 0.78 s/step; the run first had to be rescued from a design fault, see below | its own metric: mutual precision by step -- 500 0.4708, 1000 0.4742, 1500 0.4924, 2000 0.4883, 2500 0.5023, 3000 0.4842, 3500 0.5033, against the frozen version's 0.4944 and a base of 0.44; pick crossed the retriever at step 2500 (0.4821 against 0.4803) and R@1 at the same point. In the FULL cost matrix on 8 boards the same model gives edge precision 0.467 against the retriever's 0.449, and trusted island coverage 0.124 against 0.154. Inside the agreement scheme: trusted coverage 0.158, unchanged, but the biggest CLEAN island grows from 11.5 to 19.4 tiles and the top 15% of agreed edges build 8 islands per board at internal accuracy 1.000 | a real but small gain that does not survive the trip downstream, exactly as M157 found for the frozen version: a second stage rewrites only the top-k of each row, so it improves a shortlist-restricted metric far more than the cost matrix a solver consumes. Trusted coverage is now 0.154/0.158/0.159/0.158 under four unrelated schemes -- loop closure, two-model agreement, seven-model vote, and this -- which is as clear as an information ceiling gets. ENGINEERING, and it cost 45 minutes: the loop called backward once per chunk with retain_graph, so each of about 140 chunks re-traversed the whole trunk graph AND kept it alive; the run sat at 7.7 GiB of 8 and did not finish 100 steps in 35 minutes. Fixed with a two-phase backward -- chunks run against a detached leaf and free their own graphs, their gradients accumulate in leaf.grad, and the trunk is traversed exactly once via feats.backward(leaf.grad). 0.78 s/step at a 3.9 GiB peak
M165 | Pasting an island's TEXTURE without its colour | KEY / GIVES A DIAL | 20 held-out boards; instead of pasting the tile, keep the FIELD's local colour in that cell and add alpha times the tile's deviation from its own mean, so a misplaced island contributes wrong but zero-mean detail | with OUR placement: field alone +0.0196 at detail 0.3; raw paste -0.0241 at 18.5; texture alpha=1.0 -0.0214 at 13.7; 0.7 -0.0152 at 9.9; 0.5 -0.0085 at 7.2; 0.3 +0.0016 at 4.3. With TRUE placement the ordering INVERTS: raw +0.0813 at 25.5 against texture +0.0642 at 19.1 | a misplaced island hurts twice, by foreign texture and by a foreign average colour, and the second is the more expensive: removing it recovers 0.003 to 0.026 depending on alpha, and at alpha 0.3 the arm rises ABOVE the flat fill while still carrying visible structure. The control that confirms the mechanism is the inversion -- when the position is right the colour is right too, and discarding it costs 0.017. Practical result: there is now a continuous dial between the blob at +0.0196 and visible islands at -0.024, priced at about 0.0012 SSIM per unit of high-pass detail, and the choice of operating point is a judgement about what a submission should look like rather than a measurement
M166 | Is the 0.155 ceiling the edges or the BUILDER? | KEY / RENAMES THE TARGET | synthetic edge sets of controlled count and precision fed to islands_from_edges, 6 trials each; our real best is about 244 edges at 0.84 | 244 edges: precision 1.00 -> trusted coverage 0.295, 0.90 -> 0.181, 0.84 -> 0.131, 0.70 -> 0.055. 400 edges: 1.00 -> 0.622 with a biggest island of 52 tiles, 0.90 -> 0.285, 0.84 -> 0.190. 600 at 0.84 -> 0.159. 900 at 0.90 -> 0.021 | the builder is not the problem -- on real edges it returns 0.135 before growth where synthetic edges of the same count and purity give 0.131, so it extracts what the material allows and a little more. What the sweep DOES show is that the ceiling belongs to the current edge set, not to the information: 400 edges at 0.90 would take trusted coverage from 0.158 to 0.285. It also shows why taking more edges backfires -- at 900 the surplus wrong ones glue everything into a single bad island and coverage collapses to 0.02. The target is now a number: about 400 edges per board at precision 0.88
M167 | Costs from RESTORED tiles, as a second view | KEY / BEST EDGE PRECISION IN THE PROJECT | 8 held-out boards; the same matcher run over tiles passed through each of three per-tile restorers, compared and intersected with the raw view | restored alone: tile_restorer_seam 562 edges at precision 0.199, tile_restorer_mgc 690 at 0.276, tile_restorer 735 at 0.321, against the raw view's 681 at 0.449. Where raw and restored AGREE: 130 at 0.714, 227 at 0.714, 312 at 0.656; on the confident half of each: 65 at 0.949, 113 at 0.953, 156 at 0.917 | 0.95 is the highest edge precision this project has reached; the seven-model ensemble topped out at 0.89 and two-matcher agreement at 0.84. The reason is a different AXIS of diversity: models trained on the same input make correlated mistakes, while one matcher reading a raw tile and the same matcher reading a denoised tile fail in different places, because restoration removes noise and invents detail. Note the restored view is individually much WORSE than raw, exactly as M91 found -- its value is entirely in where it disagrees
M168 | Voting across input representations | PARTIAL | 8 boards, seven views: two matchers on raw tiles, three restorations, the joint scorer, MGC | views agreeing 1/2/3/4/5/6/7 -> 2493 edges at 0.165, 800 at 0.425, 523 at 0.564, 322 at 0.700, 192 at 0.820, 83 at 0.874, 10 at 0.980. Ranked by votes then margin, the top 200 run at 0.849 and give trusted coverage 0.129 | requiring every view to agree buys precision by destroying count, which is the wrong operation when M166 asks for 400 edges at 0.88. The curve is the same count-versus-precision frontier every scheme has traced
M169 | Union of several high-precision detectors | REJECTED / THE DETECTORS OVERLAP | 8 boards; each pairwise agreement (raw against a restoration, raw against the joint scorer) treated as its own detector and the unions measured | raw+restorer_mgc 113.6 edges at 0.945, raw+restorer 156.1 at 0.917, raw+restorer_seam 65.1 at 0.938, raw+joint 244.8 at 0.845. Union of the first two: 165.6 at 0.912 -- against 270 if they were disjoint. All three restorers: 169.1 at 0.907. Everything including the joint scorer: 261.9 at 0.828. Islands: all three restorers gives trusted coverage 0.144 with a biggest clean island of 17.8, against 0.158 and 15.5 for the previous best | the detectors are not complementary, they find the same easy edges: 113 plus 156 union to 165, so the overlap is nearly total. That is the count-versus-precision frontier expressing itself again, and it is why no amount of ensembling reaches 400 at 0.88. The islands do get bigger -- 17.8 tiles clean against 15.5 -- at slightly lower coverage
M170 | Three-view matcher: raw, normalised and RESTORED | REJECTED AT EQUAL BUDGET | src/seam_embed.py gained a `restored` flag adding the per-tile restorer's output as a third input view; trained 1500 steps against the identical two-view baseline | R@1 by step -- three-view 0.1973 / 0.2210 / 0.2303 at 500/1000/1500; two-view baseline 0.1861 / 0.2239 / 0.2346 | the restored view helps only at the start, where a denoised tile is easier for the early filters, and the advantage is gone by 1500 steps. Fusing the views INSIDE the model is therefore worth nothing, while intersecting the two views' cost matrices is worth a great deal (M167: precision 0.95). The information lives in the DISAGREEMENT between the views, which an intersection reads and a shared trunk averages away. Caveat: equal budget only, a long run is untested
M171 | Our edges through the earlier team's component packer | KEY / FIRST MOVEMENT IN place_acc | found by reading pazzle-fixed-orientation-20260813: R10-A packs connected components onto the board, fills every remaining cell and returns a FULL 576-tile bijection with fixed orientation, and recovers the oracle placement exactly on oracle scores. It was rejected at G2 because its selection objective, the raw rank96 logit sum, is misaligned with SSIM -- their own note says the spatial hypothesis was not refuted and that the next lever must alter candidate compatibility. Ours does: components built from raw-versus-restored agreement at precision 0.91-0.95 instead of 96 buddy edges. 8 held-out boards, about 165 agreed edges giving 55 components | place_acc: relaxation 0.0011, their buddies solver 0.0015, packer with OUR edges 0.0128. Adjacency 0.2103 / 0.2262 / 0.1827. Raw SSIM 0.0961 / 0.0998 / 0.1021. After R5+NLM 0.2145 / 0.2176 / 0.2172. All arms produce a valid bijection | place_acc moves 11-fold, the first time absolute placement has moved at all, and the output is a full unaltered 576-fragment layout, which is what the organisers require. SSIM gains only +0.003 because adjacency drops as placement rises. Two further findings: 16 restarts score WORSE than one, confirming their diagnosis that the raw logit objective is misaligned; and selecting restarts by OUR trusted edges changes nothing, because every trusted edge lies INSIDE a component and is satisfied by any packing that keeps components intact -- the objective is constant over exactly the choice being made
M172 | Literature sweep, August 2026 | REFERENCE | four searches plus the corrupted-puzzle benchmark, VLHSA and GANzzle++ | the 2025 benchmark of content-based solvers under corruption confirms our own reading: at maximum content erosion the heuristics -- Gallagher, Paikin-Tal, Yu -- misplace every piece, and the most robust method is Positional Diffusion FINE-TUNED on the corruption, whose advantage the authors attribute to iterative refinement. GANzzle++ (Pattern Recognition Letters 187, 2025) is the leading global method and its mechanism is exactly the one we built and measured: generate a 'mental image' of the solution from the pieces, then Hungarian-assign pieces to it. M140 priced that at our corruption -- with an ORACLE field, per-tile assignment reaches place_acc 0.038 -- so the state of the art in global assignment is dead here for a reason that is about our noise level, not their method. VLHSA (Sept 2025) reports +14.2 points of piece accuracy on eroded gaps by substituting TEXT descriptions for boundary similarity, which needs an input we do not have | nothing in the literature addresses corruption as heavy as this task's (sigma 40-55 additive noise plus per-tile affine plus JPEG 35-50); the published erosions are saturation, contrast and paint flaking. The transferable idea is VLHSA's: when boundaries are gone, semantics is the substitute -- and M141 already showed the semantics survive here (a tile is identified among 576 at 0.66) while adjacency does not
M173 | Filling the untrusted cells by colour continuity | PARTIAL | src/fill_smooth.py; after the packer places components, the remaining ~350 cells are filled most-constrained-first with the leftover tile whose mean colour best matches its already-placed neighbours, instead of by a seam score M160 measured at precision 0.349 for exactly those tiles; 10 held-out boards | raw SSIM: relaxation 0.1021, packer 0.1075, packer plus smooth fill 0.1095, and -- the control -- smooth fill ALONE, with no components at all, 0.1046 at adjacency 0.0070. After R5+NLM: 0.2286 / 0.2303 / 0.2290 / 0.2201 | arranging tiles purely by colour continuity, with essentially zero correct adjacencies, beats our seam-based solver on RAW SSIM. That is M137 restated at the level of the layout itself: the metric is paid by the colour field, and a solver optimising seams is optimising something the metric barely sees. The post-processing then reverses the ranking, which is the subject of M174
M174 | Restoration erases the layout | KEY / EXPLAINS EVERY SOLVER RESULT IN THIS PROJECT | 6 held-out boards, four layouts spanning adjacency 0.0054 to 0.2115, each carried through the same restoration ladder | adjacency 0.2115 / 0.1851 / 0.1529 / 0.0054 gives raw SSIM 0.0983 / 0.0972 / 0.0981 / 0.0963; after R5+NLM 0.2159 / 0.2115 / 0.2092 / 0.2027; at 75% restoration 0.2862 / 0.2837 / 0.2813 / 0.2805; at full restoration plus NLM 0.3436 / 0.3438 / 0.3435 / 0.3433 | at the operating point that scores best, the entire spread between our best solver and a layout with NO correct adjacencies is 0.0005. The layout contributes about 0.005 at moderate restoration and essentially nothing at full restoration, because the restoration removes precisely the high frequencies the layout controls. This is why every solver improvement in this project has been worth hundredths: they were measured through a post-processing that destroys them. Consequence for the submission: the metric cannot distinguish our layouts, so the layout should be chosen for being an honest assembly and the restoration strength for surviving manual review -- neither choice is a metric decision
M175 | Training the restorer under a DETAIL FLOOR | REJECTED / THE BLEND IS THE RIGHT MECHANISM | src/train_restore_ours.py gained a one-sided penalty on the output's high-pass spread, so the network is asked for texture only when it falls below the floor; 5000 steps at floor 9, against the post-hoc blend of R5 and the fitted restorer at equal texture | trained with the floor: gain over flat -0.1412 at detail 10.1 (step 1000), -0.1263 at 9.4, -0.1219 at 9.5, -0.1089 at 10.0 (step 3000). The blend at detail 8.6 scores -0.0714 | the constraint works -- the floor holds -- and the result is worse, for a reason worth keeping: forced to produce texture, the network INVENTS it, and invented texture is uncorrelated with the target and charged for at the rate M145 measured. The blend's texture is the real fragment content arriving through R5, so it is partially correlated wherever the layout is right. A one-parameter interpolation between a faithful denoiser and a fitted smoother already traces the Pareto frontier, and a network trained under a constraint does not beat it

M176 | Restoring hard inside components and gently outside | ACCEPTED, SMALL / AND IT DATES THE COMPONENTS | scratchpad/masked_blend.py, 12 held-out boards. R5's faithful output inside the cells our components own, the fitted smoother outside, mask softened with a Gaussian; against the global blend at EQUAL detail, which is the only fair comparison M145 allows | components own 142-240 cells per board (mean 204 of 576). Hard mask: 0.2491 at detail 28.7 where the global dial reads about 0.2295 at that detail, +0.020. Soft mask sigma 9: 0.2775 at detail 18.1 against about 0.2715, +0.006. The arm masked by TRUE placement collapses to detail 1.9 and 0.3435 -- indistinguishable from full restoration | the masked blend is a real if modest win and costs nothing at inference. The oracle arm is the finding: it degenerates because the true mask is nearly EMPTY. Of 576 cells the number holding their true fragment runs 0, 1, 0, 0, 2, 0, 0, 52, 1, 10, 0, 6. Components built at edge precision 0.95 are correct texture in the wrong place -- they are right about who touches whom and wrong about where. Any scheme that spends its confidence on absolute cell identity is spending it on something we do not have

M177 | Placing all 576 fragments to match a colour field, by Hungarian assignment | MECHANISM CONFIRMED, FIELD IS THE BOTTLENECK | scratchpad/field_assign.py, 12 held-out boards. Cost is the squared distance between a cell's predicted colour and a fragment's mean colour, solved exactly on 576x576; every arm is a full bijection of unaltered fragments. Fragment means taken raw and after the per-tile restorers, against the predicted field and against the true 24x24 downsample | flat fill 0.3552; field drawn 0.3814 (+0.026, no fragments); packer 0.2862 at place 0.0104; predicted field + assignment 0.3007 at place 0.0010, BELOW chance 0.0017; ORACLE field + assignment 0.3711 at place 0.0367, twenty-one times chance, and above the flat fill. Restored fragment means beat raw means by 0.0003, nothing | the assignment mechanism recovers absolute position from colour alone at twenty-one times chance and yields a conformant board above the constant -- with a true field. Our field delivers none of it, and the checkpoints say why: the blind twin, whose tile features are zeroed, already scores +0.0075 of the field's +0.0114, so two thirds of the field is a generic photograph prior and the bag of tiles is worth +0.0039. This closes M161's negative result properly: island placement by field failed because the FIELD is empty, not because colour is the wrong cue

M178 | The number that reframes the project | MEASUREMENT | same run, same 12 boards | the flat fill scores 0.3552 here. Our best conformant arm scores 0.2862 at blend 0.5 and 0.3389 at full restoration. Every submission this project has produced is BELOW a constant image, and the platform agrees: deployed 0.23748 against a leader at 0.40 which is itself only about +0.02 over the constant | at full restoration the network emits a smooth image whose low frequencies are the local means of a SCRAMBLED board. That structure is uncorrelated with the target, and SSIM charges for uncorrelated structure at the rate M145 measured, while a constant carries no structure and pays nothing. The consequence is a construction rather than a complaint: the high frequencies should come from the fragments, which are real content and the only part that looks restored, and the low frequencies from the best independent estimate of the colour map. Nobody in this competition has assembled the puzzle -- a correct assembly scores 0.6 to 0.7, and the leader is at 0.40

M179 | Levelling the per-fragment brightness from the seams, and shrinking the field | LEVELLING ACCEPTED / SHRINKAGE REJECTED | src/level_seams.py solves, per channel, the 1104-seam least-squares system whose unknowns are one additive offset per fragment, regularised toward zero and re-centred on the board mean -- panorama gain compensation, applied after assembly, needing no knowledge of the layout. Priced inside the composition `field + alpha * highpass(assembly)`, 12 held-out boards | flat fill 0.3552. Packer, plain R5+NLM: 0.2270 raw against 0.2528 levelled. Packer, composed at alpha 0.25: 0.3387 raw against 0.3490 levelled, and at alpha 0.4: 0.3115 against 0.3271, about +0.005 once matched on detail. Best conformant arm to date: field-assigned layout, levelled, alpha 0.25 -- 0.3509 at detail 5.5, which is flat -0.0043 where the previously built submission sits at flat -0.069. Field shrunk toward flat at 0.7 LOSES 0.010; blurring the field at sigma 40 changes nothing (0.3492 against 0.3490) | levelling is free, needs no layout, is exactly the restoration the organisers name, and removes the one piece of low-frequency structure on the board that is certainly wrong. That shrinkage hurts and heavy blur is a no-op says the field is already conservative to the point of being nearly featureless -- its error is not overconfidence, it is absence of signal

M180 | Does the object-size effect hold for the components we actually build | REJECTED / THE BOOTSTRAP IS CLOSED | scratchpad/merge_precision.py, 12 boards. For every internally-correct component, the best of all placements against all other components by mean seam cost, checked against the true relative offset | 635 components, 498 internally correct (0.784). Top-1 precision 0.145 overall, 0.265 on mutual-best pairs, 0.367 on the most confident tenth by margin. Precision does NOT rise with size -- 2 tiles 0.154, 4 tiles 0.207, 6 or more 0.169 -- nor with seam length: 2 tiles 0.147, 3 tiles 0.151, 4 tiles 0.000 of 10 | M150 measured synthetic k x k blocks retrieved from a set of 576 candidates. Real components face a different problem: 498 components times hundreds of admissible offsets is a candidate set two orders of magnitude larger, and the reliability a longer seam buys is more than spent on it. Contacts are short as well -- 434 of 498 proposals are judged on a two-tile seam. Agglomerative growth cannot bootstrap here, and one fifth of the components are already internally wrong before any merge is attempted

M181 | The operating point, on 24 boards, with everything switched on | THE FIRST CONFORMANT ARM ABOVE THE FLAT FILL | scratchpad/final_recipe.py. Three full bijections -- the packer's seam-driven layout, the same with the leftover cells assigned by colour to the field instead of by the seam score M160 measured at precision 0.349, and a colour assignment of all 576 -- each levelled and composed as field + alpha * highpass at sigma 20 | flat fill 0.3525, field drawn 0.3688 (+0.0162). At alpha 0.15: packer 0.3534 (+0.0008) at detail 3.7, packer with the field fill 0.3548 (+0.0023) at detail 3.4, colour assignment 0.3541 (+0.0016) at detail 3.3. At alpha 0.25 all three fall to about -0.013, at alpha 0.5 to about -0.045. Placement: packer 0.0060, packer with field fill 0.0064, colour assignment 0.0012 against chance 0.0017; adjacency 0.176, 0.147 and 0.006 | for the first time a submission that places every fragment scores above the constant. The three layouts are within 0.0015 of each other, so the choice is not a metric choice: the packer's layout has the highest adjacency and is the one that assembles from evidence rather than from a prediction. Replacing the seam fill with a colour fill is worth +0.0015 and costs 0.03 of adjacency -- it buys the score by making the board LESS assembled, which is the wrong trade under a manual review. The gap between the conformant arms and the field drawn alone, about 0.014, is the price of showing the fragments at all

M182 | At which spatial scale should the surviving texture live | SIGMA 20 CONFIRMED, HYPOTHESIS REJECTED | scratchpad/sigma_sweep.py, 24 boards, packer layout, levelled. Alpha in {0.20, 0.35, 0.50, 0.70} crossed with the high-pass cut-off sigma in {3, 6, 10, 20} px. The hypothesis was that SSIM's 11-pixel window charges less for structure much finer than itself, so a small sigma would buy visible grain cheaply | at EQUAL alpha a smaller sigma always scores higher and always shows less detail, which is not a frontier result. At equal detail the order reverses cleanly. Interpolating each sigma's curve at detail 5.0: sigma 3 gives 0.3348, sigma 6 gives 0.3399, sigma 10 gives 0.3435, sigma 20 gives 0.3458. Sigma 40, measured earlier, is worse than 20 at matched detail | the cut-off belongs at one fragment width and the earlier reading that favoured small sigma was an artefact of comparing at fixed alpha instead of fixed detail. The frontier itself is the finding: it is monotone from the field alone down to full texture, with no knee anywhere. There is no operating point that is cheap -- every unit of visible texture costs score at a steady rate, so the choice of alpha is a decision about what the picture should look like and nothing else

M183 | Which texture source buys the most score per unit of visible detail | EDGE-PRESERVING PASS ACCEPTED | scratchpad/texture_source.py, 16 boards, same levelled assembly, alpha swept and the arms compared on the frontier of SSIM against detail rather than at fixed alpha. Not all surviving texture is content: residual noise and JPEG blocking are uncorrelated by construction, real fragment content is partly correlated wherever the layout is right, so a source with a higher content fraction should be cheaper | flat fill 0.3511. At alpha 0.5: R5 alone 0.2880 at detail 13.2, R5+NLM 0.3094 at 12.2, R5+NLM then a bilateral pass (d 7, sigma_colour 40, sigma_space 7) 0.3130 at 12.0, and restoring each fragment BEFORE assembly 0.2956 at 14.5. Matched on detail the bilateral pass is worth +0.003 at detail 12 and +0.004 at detail 17 | the edge-preserving pass is free, is exactly the artefact removal the organisers name, and keeps fragment boundaries and text while deleting grain. Restoring fragments before assembly loses: the per-tile restorers invent detail with no context, and R5 on the assembled board sees 20 pixels of neighbourhood that they do not

M184 | What the seam levelling is worth where it can be checked | STRONG, AND IT SCALES WITH THE ASSEMBLY | scratchpad/level_quality.py, 12 boards, CPU only. On the TRUE layout the question is well posed: the generator's total photometric shift per fragment is exactly mean(dirty) - mean(clean), which the targets give. Offsets from src/level_seams.py, and an alternating least squares that solves offsets and a per-fragment GAIN together | the true shift has a spread of 33.91 grey levels; after the seam offsets 20.29, so 64% of its variance is removed, at correlation 0.810 with the truth. On a correctly assembled board: raw 0.4560 at detail 37.8, with offsets 0.4994 at 28.9, with offsets and gains 0.5077 at 26.0. Gains are worth +0.0083 over offsets alone even though their correlation with the offset truth is slightly lower (0.790), because they correct contrast, which that correlation does not measure | +0.052 on a correct assembly is the largest single restoration gain measured in this project and it needs no learning at all. It also scales with the solver: every point of assembly accuracy makes the seam evidence more truthful, so this is one of the few things that would multiply if the puzzle were ever solved. A correct assembly levelled scores 0.5077 against a flat fill at 0.3525 and the ORACLE field drawn at 0.4798 -- the assembly is worth more than a perfect colour map, which is the reassurance that solving remains the right goal

M185 | Solving for per-fragment GAIN as well as offset | CORRECT ON A CORRECT LAYOUT, DEGENERATE ON OURS -- DO NOT SHIP | scratchpad/level_wrong.py, 8 boards, CPU. The same alternating least squares as M184, run on a RANDOM layout, which stands in fairly for ours since we place about six fragments in 576 | the solved gains have mean 0.745 and spread 0.484, and 85% of them fall outside [0.6, 1.6] -- the generator never draws contrast outside [0.70, 1.30]. SSIM rises from 0.1017 to 0.1562 while detail falls from 31.3 to 21.1 | the only way to make a seam that was never continuous look continuous is to crush the contrast on both sides of it, so the solve saturates its clip and the score rises for the forbidden reason. This is the M182 lesson in a second costume: a knob that raises SSIM by removing texture has not been shown to help. Offsets stay, gains are gated off in `level_seams.level` and documented as such -- they become correct exactly when the assembly does

M186 | Fusing the raw and restored views as CALIBRATED PROBABILITIES rather than as a filter | REJECTED | scratchpad/view_fusion.py, 8 boards. M167 found the two views err in different places and used that as a filter keeping about 113 edges; if the errors really are independent the calibrated posteriors should be multiplied instead, which would raise precision over the whole matrix. Each view Sinkhorned separately, then summed or min-ed, then cycle consistency, exactly as the deployed path treats one view | raw only 0.449 precision at 681 edges, R@1 0.309. Restored only 0.276 at 690. Sum of logits 0.303 at 943; sum after Sinkhorn 0.305 at 942; min after Sinkhorn 0.351 at 741. At a fixed budget of 750 edges: 0.453 raw against 0.354, 0.355, 0.360 | the restored view is half as informative as the raw one, so fusion dilutes rather than reinforces. M167's finding survives only in its original form -- as an intersection that identifies a subset where both happen to be right. Independence of errors is not the same as equality of information, and only the second justifies a product

M187 | Recall at depth for the CURRENT matcher: the number M26 never took | KEY / REOPENS THE SOLVER AXIS | scratchpad/recall_depth.py, 8 boards, deployed cost path, and a twin-tolerant variant at the 10 grey-level threshold M83 uses | logits alone R@1/5/20/64/128 = 0.247/0.436/0.618/0.791/0.887. Deployed path 0.309/0.486/0.656/0.812/0.894. Twin-tolerant on the deployed path 0.356/0.596/0.782/0.905/0.956. MGC, which is what M26 measured, reads 0.159/0.328/0.512/0.680 | M26's "a third of fragments lack the true neighbour even in the top 64, so no solver can recover them" is a statement about MGC and it does not hold for the current matcher: the true neighbour is in the top 20 for two thirds of tiles and in the top 64 for four fifths. THE INFORMATION IS PRESENT AND THE PROBLEM IS RANKING. A perfect picker inside the top 5 alone would give R@1 0.486, which is past the 0.47 where M102 saw placement switch on

M188 | The two switched-off knobs in joint_cost.py | REJECTED / M164's VERDICT CONFIRMED TWICE | scratchpad/joint_sweep.py, 8 boards, seam_joint_v1. Knob one: `rounds > 0`, re-running Sinkhorn and cycle consistency AFTER the joint head has spoken, which its own docstring calls "a different thing" and leaves off -- consistency is the mechanism that propagates a local gain across the matrix. Knob two: k = 64 at inference, which M106 predicted would buy 0.15 of shortlist ceiling for inference cost alone | retriever baseline R@1 0.308 / 682 edges / precision 0.450. Every joint arm lands between 0.437 and 0.467: k=20 blend 1.0 rounds 0 gives 0.463 at 650 edges; rounds 1 raises edges to 697 and precision falls to 0.461; rounds 3 gives 734 edges at 0.446. k=64 is indistinguishable from k=20 throughout | the joint head's +0.06 on its own shortlist metric is genuinely local. Re-running consistency buys edges by lowering the bar and loses exactly the precision it gains in count, and a deeper shortlist adds nothing because the head cannot rank what it could not rank at depth 20. Three re-rankers and two post-hoc knobs now agree: pairwise re-scoring is finished as a route

M189 | A 2x2 verifier on pixels, trained BINARY | REJECTED / THE OBJECTIVE WAS THE CEILING | src/train_plaquette_hardneg.py trains plaquette.PlaquetteNet (2.0M parameters) on true 2x2 patches against hard negatives drawn from the matcher's own top-8 shortlists, 400 boards, 8000 steps, 11 minutes, best balanced accuracy 0.748. Evaluated in scratchpad/eval_plaquette.py against the sum of the four seam scores, on candidate quads built exactly as a harvest would: the anchor's top-5 right and top-5 down, and for each pair the two best fourth tiles by consistency | the true quad is among the 50 candidates for 0.256 of anchors. Pick accuracy: pairwise sum 0.143 overall and 0.560 of covered; the verifier 0.045 and 0.177; the two combined 0.127. By yield, precision at 0.05/0.1/0.2 is 0.697/0.532/0.429 for the pairwise sum and 0.128/0.118/0.086 for the verifier | a separation model predicted the result exactly before it was run: a scorer with AUC near 0.8 picks one of fifty correctly 17% of the time, and 0.177 came back. So the architecture is not the limit, the training question is -- binary training asks "is this quad real" and the harvest asks "which of these fifty is". Second fault, and M107 wrote it down already: a second stage must not see LESS than the stage it corrects, and this one sees pixels where the pairwise sum sees the matcher's learned scores. Both are fixed in the listwise residual version: softmax over the real candidate set, and the pairwise sum ADDED rather than replaced, with the correction's weight learned from zero so the model starts exactly at the baseline

M190 | Four matcher checkpoints exist and the deployed one is the weakest | SMALL FREE GAIN / METHODOLOGICAL | scratchpad/matcher_bakeoff.py, 8 boards, identical calibrated cost path for each. seam_embed_v1 is what every path in this repo loads; v1b, v2 and mix sit beside it in the checkpoint directory and are mentioned NOWHERE in this log. v2 was stopped at step 20000 of a planned 40000, v1 at 17000 of 30000 | training-loop R@1, which scores raw cosines: v1 0.2488, mix 0.3166, v1b 0.3196, v2 0.3277. On the full calibrated matrix the ordering survives but the gap does not -- R@1 0.309/0.310/0.312/0.321 and mutual edge precision 0.449/0.441/0.459/0.467, with v2 best on both. Recall at depth barely moves: R@64 0.812 against 0.818 | +0.018 edge precision for a checkpoint swap is worth taking and is nowhere near the 0.72 knee. The methodological point is the larger one and M99 predicted it: the training loop's number understates the calibrated path so badly that a checkpoint looking 32% better in the log is 4% better in the matrix, which is how three trained matchers came to sit unmeasured in a directory. Any future checkpoint claim must be read on the full matrix or not at all

M191 | Listwise training, and a metric that scored its own trap | ENGINEERING / METHODOLOGICAL | the corrected objective for the 2x2 verifier, src/train_plaquette_listwise.py | the first attempt gave a NONE class to anchors whose true quad is absent from the candidate set, and reported "pick 0.7639" at cover 0.2361 after 500 steps. 1 - 0.2361 = 0.7639 exactly: the model had learned to answer NONE always, and the metric counted every abstention as a win | two lessons. A score that cannot exceed coverage must be REPORTED against coverage, or a degenerate policy reads as a triumph -- the arithmetic identity was the only thing that caught it. And abstention belongs in a threshold, not in a class, when the abstain case is three quarters of the data: training now runs on covered anchors only and the harvest accepts by margin, which is also how the pairwise baseline is used, so the comparison stays like for like. Second engineering note: rejection-sampling covered anchors per step cost 2.6 s of CPU per step; computing the covered set once, vectorised, took it to 0.25 s

M192 | The residual gate that was not a gate | ENGINEERING / TRAP 1 AGAIN | src/train_plaquette_listwise.py scored `pairwise_sum + w * plaquette(quad)` with w a learned scalar initialised at zero, so that the model would start exactly at the baseline and could only earn its way above it | after 1000 steps w read -0.025, the loss had moved from 2.1670 to 2.1578, and the pick rate sat at 0.5556 against the baseline's 0.5694 -- below the thing it started from | this repo's oldest trap, written down as trap 1 and walked into anyway: a zero-initialised gate on a signal the model can ignore stays at zero. The network's gradient is scaled by the gate, so at w = 0 it receives nothing; the gate's own gradient needs the network to already be useful, which a randomly initialised network is not. The fix is to add the correction at unit weight, standardised per anchor so the two terms are commensurable. The model then starts BELOW the baseline -- 0.434 against 0.569, which is the honest starting point for an untrained correction -- and the loss falls from the first step. Design rule: a residual that must be LEARNED cannot be introduced through a multiplicative gate on itself

M193 | Does the four-corner junction carry anything the four seams do not | REJECTED / CLOSES THE 2x2 ROUTE | src/train_plaquette_listwise.py, softmax over the 50 candidates the harvest actually builds, correction added at unit weight without a gate (M192), 270 train boards, best validation at step 4500. Evaluated in scratchpad/eval_plaquette_lw.py by SWEEPING the mixing weight, which is the kind form of the question: a weak scorer is still worth mixing at a small weight if its errors sit elsewhere | pick of covered anchors by mixing weight: 0.00 -> 0.560, 0.15 -> 0.557, 0.30 -> 0.553, 0.50 -> 0.536, 0.75 -> 0.518, 1.00 -> 0.502. Precision at yield 0.05 falls the same way, 0.393 to 0.351. The verifier alone picks 0.022, which is chance for a 50-way choice. Training peaked at 0.5174 against the pairwise sum's 0.5694 and then FELL to 0.5000 by step 6000 while the loss kept dropping | zero is the optimal weight at every yield and the degradation is monotone, so the junction adds nothing. This was the one piece of evidence in the problem that no pairwise score can reach, and a 2.0M-parameter CNN reading the assembled 40x40 patch cannot extract it. Note the honest caveat: the run overfits 270 boards before reaching the baseline, so a larger-data retrain is the one untested variant -- but a signal that degrades monotonically as it is mixed in is not one that more data usually rescues

M194 | Standardising a score destroys the ranking BETWEEN anchors | METHODOLOGICAL | noticed comparing two evaluations of the same pairwise sum on the same boards | ranked by the raw margin, precision at yield 0.05 is 0.697; ranked by the margin of the per-anchor standardised score, 0.393 | per-anchor standardisation is necessary to make two scorers commensurable inside a softmax, and it is exactly wrong for deciding WHICH anchors to accept, because it throws away the between-anchor scale that makes a confident anchor confident. Both operations are needed and they must not share a number: standardise for training, rank the harvest on the raw margin

M195 | If re-ranking were PERFECT, would the solvers switch on | KEY / VALIDATES THE FRAMING AND EXPOSES A SECOND GAP | scratchpad/oracle_rerank.py, 6 boards. The oracle re-ranker needs no training: in every row whose true neighbour is already inside the top K, move it to rank one, and leave every other row exactly as the matcher left it -- the best any re-ranker restricted to a depth-K shortlist can do. Then the deployed component packer, measured on edges, placement and adjacency | matcher 675 edges at precision 0.462, place 0.0014, adjacency 0.180, 36.5 components. Oracle top-5: 716 edges at 0.732, place 0.0509, adjacency 0.308. Top-20: 786 at 0.883, place 0.0770, adjacency 0.436. Top-64: 886 at 0.954, place 0.2144, adjacency 0.508. Full oracle: 1024 at 0.995, place 0.4294, adjacency 0.694, 2.2 components | the framing is validated and the target is now exact: a perfect choice inside the top FIVE lands on 0.732, which is M102's knee, and takes placement from 0.0014 to 0.0509, thirty times chance. We currently choose correctly inside the top five 63.6% of the time. The second reading is the one nobody has priced: at edge precision 0.995 the packer still places only 43% correctly and builds the board as 2.2 components -- it assembles almost the whole picture as one piece and puts that piece in the wrong place. Even a perfect matcher leaves more than half the placement on the table, so the origin problem (M108) is not a side issue, it is the second of two gaps of comparable size

M196 | Choosing the whole board's origin by the colour field | REJECTED / CLOSES ORIGIN AT BOARD SCALE, AND CORRECTS M195 | scratchpad/origin_by_field.py, 8 boards, deployed harvest (raw-and-restored agreement, component packer). The layout is never re-ordered, only translated over all 576 shifts; each shift's 24x24 tile-mean map is compared with the predicted field, by level and by spatial gradient | the packer's own origin places 0.0119. Field by colour 0.0028, field by gradient 0.0022, both far WORSE than doing nothing. The oracle shift reaches 0.0406, and it lands on (0, 0) on one board of eight | M108 closed origin recovery and M161 closed placing islands by the field; this closes the remaining variant, a 576-way choice over complete board hypotheses, which was the best-conditioned form the question has. The field cannot even beat the packer's incidental choice. Correction to M195: the factor of forty quoted there was measured on the raw mutual-edge pipeline, not the deployed harvest. On the deployed harvest origin is worth 3.4x (0.0119 to 0.0406), which is real and much smaller than that reading suggested

M197 | Finishing the matcher's own recipe | SMALL GAIN / CLOSES THE SCALING LEVER | seam_embed_v2 was v1 warm-started and was stopped at step 20000 of a planned 40000. Continued with the same architecture and predict-weight 0.3 at a lower peak lr (1.5e-4) for 20000 more steps, 70 minutes at 0.21 s/step, then baked off against v1 and v2 on 10 boards through the full calibrated path | the training metric climbed steadily: R@1 0.3265 at init, 0.3271 at 2500, 0.3280 at 5000, 0.3318 at 7500, 0.3324 at 10000, 0.3354 at 12500, 0.3365 at 15000, 0.3386 at 17500, 0.3365 at 20000. R@20 never moved -- 0.660, 0.659, 0.660, 0.663, 0.665, 0.663, 0.667, 0.666. On the full matrix: v1 precision 0.438 at R@1 0.299, v2 0.458 at 0.311, v3 0.460 at 0.320 | twenty thousand steps bought +0.002 edge precision over v2. R@20 standing still through the whole run is the diagnostic: the shortlist is saturated and only the ordering inside it improves, which is exactly the quantity calibration already recovers. M190's compression repeats -- +0.009 of training R@1 becomes +0.002 of matrix precision. Total across three checkpoints, v1 to v3, is +0.022 against a knee at 0.72, so training longer is not the lever

M198 | The packer's default budget throws away a factor of 58 in the regime we are aiming at | KEY / FREE, AND ONE LANDMINE | scratchpad/packer_budget.py, 3 boards. `solve_components_from_scores` is called everywhere in this repo with repair_passes=0, restarts=1, its cheapest setting. Swept at three edge qualities: today's matcher, a perfect top-20 re-ranker, and a perfect matcher | today's matcher: place 0.0012 at restarts 1 and 0.0012 at restarts 3 -- below the knee nothing helps. ORACLE TOP-20: place 0.0029 at restarts 1 and 0.1672 at restarts 3, a factor of 58, for 0.3 s; best shift is unchanged at 0.327 against 0.328 and adjacency identical at 0.436, so the LAYOUT is the same and what the restarts find is the origin. Oracle full: 0.5619 either way. repair_passes=2 is destructive everywhere -- adjacency collapses from 0.436 to 0.002 and from 0.712 to 0.024 -- and costs 145 s per board | two things. The default is tuned for the regime we are in and badly wrong for the regime we are trying to reach, so the moment edge precision improves this is a free multiple sitting under it; M195's "second gap" is mostly a tuning artefact and not a fundamental limit. And repair_passes must stay at zero: it is a landmine that ruins the layout it is asked to repair, at a hundred times the cost of not calling it

M199 | What perfect photometry is worth to the LEARNED matcher | REJECTED / CLOSES THE PHOTOMETRIC LINE AND CORRECTS A CARRIED BELIEF | scratchpad/photometry_gate.py, 8 boards, seam_embed_v3, full calibrated path. The clean tiles are available in training, so the exact per-fragment photometry is inverted by least squares, `dirty ~ a * clean + b` per fragment per channel, and fed back | as delivered R@1 0.330 at edge precision 0.471. Offset removed by ORACLE: 0.256 at 0.321. Full affine removed by ORACLE: 0.316 at 0.409. Clean tiles: 0.662 at 0.802 | removing the corruption perfectly makes the matcher WORSE, at both strengths. It was trained on tiles carrying this photometry and carries a noise-aware normalised view for exactly that purpose, so undoing it puts the input out of distribution. The belief this project has carried since the first signal budget -- that the brightness spread alone halves solvability -- is true for MSE and MGC and FALSE for the learned matcher, which has already absorbed it. The photometric line is closed, including the seam-based estimator that M184 built: there is nothing left for it to recover. The reading that matters is the last row: clean tiles score 0.802, above the 0.72 knee, so what still binds is the noise, blur and JPEG, and M36 already named noise as the dominant destroyer

M200 | The local head: one descriptor per position along the seam | NO DIFFERENCE / CLOSES THE POSITIONAL HYPOTHESIS | `seam_embed.py` has carried a `head="local"` branch since it was written -- a descriptor per position along the edge, so the score sums 20 local agreements instead of comparing two summaries, which is the physically correct model of a seam and is what M107 identified as the thing a dot product cannot express. All four existing checkpoints use `head="global"` and the branch appears nowhere in 199 experiments. Trained with the v3 trunk warm-started and fresh heads, 24000 steps, 100 minutes, then run through the end-to-end gate on 8 boards | training metric: 0.3010 at step 2000 from random heads, 0.3228 at 12000, 0.3347 at 18000, 0.3330 at 24000, against v3's 0.3365. End to end: R@1 0.329 against 0.330, R@20 0.672 against 0.671, 699 edges against 698, precision 0.475 against 0.471, place 0.0087 against 0.0106, adjacency 0.200 against 0.195 | identical within noise on every quantity, with twenty times fewer head parameters and half the training budget. The global head has already learned whatever positional structure the seam carries, so the dot-product objection is real in principle and empty in practice here. The result is not wasted: v3 and local are equally strong and architecturally different, which is the SYMMETRIC case M186's fusion lacked -- there the restored view was half as informative and diluted. Fusing these two is the fair test of that idea

M201 | Pessimistic fusion of two matchers of EQUAL strength | ACCEPTED / THE FIRST FUSION HERE THAT DOES NOT LOSE | scratchpad/fuse_matchers.py. M186 fused the raw and restored views and lost because the restored view carries half the information; M200 produced a symmetric pair instead -- v3 with a flattened whole-edge head and `local` with twenty positional descriptors, 0.471 and 0.475 precision. Each matrix Sinkhorned on its own, then combined, then cycle consistency, exactly as one view is treated | on 8 boards: v3 0.471 at 698 edges, local 0.475 at 699, sum of logits 0.404 at 848, sum after Sinkhorn 0.404 at 850, MIN after Sinkhorn 0.480 at 703 with R@1 0.334 against 0.330. On 6 boards with more arms: min of 2 gives 0.496, min of 3 0.492, min of 4 0.482, min of 6 0.479, median of 6 0.484, mean of 6 0.490, against v3 alone 0.485. Unanimous mutual agreement across all six checkpoints leaves 372 edges at precision 0.693 | summing dilutes even between equals, because adding two calibrated matrices manufactures new mutual-best pairs of mediocre quality -- 150 extra edges at 0.404. The MINIMUM is the right operator: it takes the pessimistic view of every entry, keeps the count unchanged and raises precision. Adding weaker checkpoints degrades it monotonically, which restates M186's law in a cleaner form: fusion helps between equals and hurts otherwise. The unanimity filter's 372 edges at 0.693 sits ON the count-precision frontier M166 mapped, not above it. Deployed matcher to today's best: edge precision 0.438 to 0.496

M202 | Total variation as a global objective: ranks layouts, cannot find the origin | KEY / HALF ACCEPTED | scratchpad/tv_objective.py, 6 boards. The other team's `global_critic` gate records its learned critic at accuracy 0.558 against a plain total-variation baseline at 0.933, with `learned_lift_over_tv` = -0.375; by family TV scores 0.75 on an adjacent swap, 0.84 on a nearby swap and 1.00 on block swaps of 2, 4 and 6 tiles. Tested here as a layout ranker and as an origin chooser, on the raw assembled board | RANKING, TV across the fragment seams: true layout 71.78, packer 74.68, packer translated 77.67, random 133.71 -- monotone in placement accuracy. ORIGIN, over all 576 translations of the same layout: TV over the whole canvas picks a shift placing 0.0000, TV over the seams also 0.0000, against the packer's own incidental 0.0029 and an oracle shift at 0.0333 | the ranking half is real: the true layout has measurably lower TV than ours, and TV is the strongest known detector of exactly our failure mode, a large correct block sitting in the wrong place, which summed seam cost cannot see because moving a block only changes the seams on its border. The origin half fails for a reason that closes the question generally: a toroidal translation leaves every adjacency intact except the 48 seams that wrap, so 1056 of 1104 seams are IDENTICAL across all 576 shifts. The origin is nearly invisible to any seam-based objective, TV or learned, which is why M196's field and M108's three attempts all failed. Note also that TV must be read on the RAW board -- seam levelling solves for the offsets that minimise seam steps for whatever layout it is given, and would flatten the very differences being measured

M203 | Choosing among a bag of layouts, by total variation or by anything else | REJECTED / THE BAG IS EMPTY | scratchpad/tv_select.py, 6 boards, 16 layouts each. M202 showed TV orders layouts correctly and detects block misplacement at accuracy 1.00, so selection was the use that survived its failure at origin. First attempt returned 16 IDENTICAL layouts -- the packer ignores its seed on restart 0, which takes a deterministic path -- so diversity was made by jittering the costs at 0.15 of their own spread, which samples the neighbourhood of the objective's optimum | with a genuinely diverse bag: the deterministic layout that ships places 0.0029 at adjacency 0.197; chosen by the packer's own summed cost 0.0038; chosen by TV 0.0029 at adjacency 0.191; and the ORACLE best in the bag 0.0055 at adjacency 0.202 | the chooser is not the problem, the bag is. Sixteen samples around the optimum contain nothing better than 0.0055 where the deterministic answer is 0.0029, so the whole selection idea is worth at most a factor of 1.9 and no available criterion collects it. This is M112's conclusion arriving from the other side: below the knee the objective's neighbourhood does not contain the truth, so no amount of search or re-ranking downstream of the edges can help. The lever is edge precision and nothing else

M204 | What a cell's second, third and fourth neighbour are worth | KEY / QUANTIFIES THE BOOTSTRAP | scratchpad/neighbour_evidence.py, 8 boards, deployed fused cost path. For every cell, its k TRUE neighbours are revealed and all 576 tiles are scored by the sum of the k calibrated seam log-probabilities | top-1 by number of known neighbours: 1 -> 0.324, 2 -> 0.511, 3 -> 0.608, 4 -> 0.669. Top-5: 0.494, 0.663, 0.747, 0.789. Top-20: 0.649, 0.781, 0.850, 0.870 | this is M150's object-size effect taken on the EVIDENCE side instead of the object side, and that side does not pay for it: the candidate set is 576 at every k while the evidence doubles. Two correct neighbours put the choice at 0.511, above M102's 0.47 activation threshold. It also states the bootstrap exactly: correct neighbours are worth more than anything else available, and correct neighbours are the thing we do not have

M205 | Most-constrained-first growth, three seedings | REJECTED / AND IT NAMES THE REAL BOTTLENECK | src/grow_cells.py fills the empty cell with the most placed neighbours first, tie-broken by margin, so the weakest decisions are made last. Validated on ORACLE costs before anything else (place 1.0000, adjacency 1.000), then run on 6 real boards | packer 0.0029 place / 0.197 adjacency; grown from nothing 0.0009 / 0.175; grown from all components 0.0032 / 0.199; grown from the single biggest component 0.0020 / 0.171. Accuracy of decisions taken with k neighbours: 0.000 at k=1, 0.001 at k=2 -- against M204's 0.511 for the same k with TRUE neighbours | the gap between 0.511 and 0.001 is the whole result. M204's evidence only compounds when the neighbours AGREE; two tiles from different components that are each internally right but wrongly placed relative to each other supply contradictory evidence, and their sum picks noise. Seeding from one component removes the contradiction and still fails, and the per-board diagnostic says why: the biggest component holds 13 to 21 tiles and is internally WRONG on three boards of six. There is nothing reliable to grow from. The bottleneck is no longer edge precision in the abstract -- it is the absence of one trustworthy core of twenty tiles

M206 | Telling a correct component from a wrong one, without the truth | ACCEPTED / SELF-CONSISTENCY PICKS THE CORE | scratchpad/core_verify.py, 12 boards, 117 components of six or more tiles, of which 65.8% are internally correct. Three truth-free signals compared: tile count, the density of closed 2x2 cycles inside the component, and SELF-CONSISTENCY -- for each tile, whether it is still the top choice among all 576 when scored by M204's multi-neighbour sum over its IN-COMPONENT neighbours | choosing the core by size, which is what `max(comps, key=len)` does today, gives a correct core 58.3% of the time; by loop density 75.0%; by self-consistency 91.7%, eleven boards of twelve. Correlation with correctness across all components: size -0.088, loops +0.109, self-consistency +0.226 | self-consistency is a weak ranker overall and an excellent argmax, which is exactly what core selection needs -- one reliable component, not a calibrated score for all of them. The negative correlation for size is the actionable part: the pipeline currently picks its largest component and larger components are slightly MORE likely to be internally wrong, because size comes from accepting more edges. A component that confirms itself under its own evidence is the thing to trust

M207 | Gated growth from a self-consistency-verified core | REJECTED / THE OBSTRUCTION IS GEOMETRIC | scratchpad/grow_gated.py, 12 boards. The core is chosen by M206's self-consistency (12.3 tiles, correct 91.7% of the time) and a cell is filled only when it clears a minimum neighbour count and a minimum margin; growth halts when nothing qualifies, so the output is partial and is scored on coverage against the adjacency of what it filled | min 1 neighbour, no margin: coverage 1.000 at adjacency 0.021. Margin 2.0: 0.064 at 0.410. Min 2 neighbours: 0.040 at 0.477, with margin 1.0 0.029 at 0.688, with margin 3.0 0.023 at 0.890. Min 3 neighbours: 0.022 at 0.918. The core alone is 0.021 of the board | at the strict settings the mechanism reaches adjacency 0.89 to 0.92, which is the M204 regime working exactly as measured -- and coverage never exceeds the core it started from. Growth adds essentially nothing, and the reason is geometry rather than information: the frontier of a COMPACT region consists entirely of cells with ONE neighbour, and cells with two appear only at concave corners, which do not exist until something has already been placed at k=1 where accuracy is 0.324. The bootstrap does not fail for lack of evidence; it fails because the evidence that would suffice cannot be reached from a convex seed. Any future attempt must create concavity deliberately -- grow a row and a column first, then fill the rectangle they enclose -- and must find some way to make those k=1 decisions reliable, which is the same wall in a new shape

M208 | Does pessimistic fusion accumulate with a third equal member | NO / IT SATURATES AT TWO | scratchpad/fuse_matchers.py, 6 boards. A third matcher was trained for this: same trunk warm start, local head, strip 7 instead of 3, so its window on the seam is more than twice as wide. It lands equal in strength -- R@1 0.3342 against v3's 0.3365 and local's 0.3330 | alone: v3 0.485, local 0.489, wide 0.488. Pairs: v3+local 0.496, v3+wide 0.487, local+wide 0.485. All three: min 0.491, mean 0.494. Unanimous mutual agreement across three leaves 497 edges at precision 0.607 | the best pair stays the best combination and a third member of equal strength makes it slightly worse, so M201's gain is not the start of a series. The three share a trunk lineage -- wide and local were both warm-started from v3 -- which is the most likely reason their errors are not independent enough to keep compounding. A genuinely independent member would need a fresh trunk and about two hours; on this evidence that is not where the next gain is

M209 | Restoration for matching, tested in its OWN domain for the first time | NEUTRAL, NOT HARMFUL / CORRECTS M66 AND M91 | src/train_seam_embed.py gained `--restore-input`, which REPLACES the input tiles with a restorer's output in training and evaluation alike, as distinct from `--restored`, which adds them as a third view (M170, rejected). v3 fine-tuned that way for 20000 steps on tile_restorer's output, then every checkpoint given the input it was trained for, full calibrated matrix, 10 boards | v3 on raw 0.461 edge precision at R@1 0.320. Restored-domain model on restored input 0.454 at 0.312. The two mismatches: v3 on restored 0.335 at 0.251 -- which is M91's experiment -- and the restored-domain model on raw 0.317 at 0.212. Fusing the two domains gives 0.465 against 0.467 for min v3+local on raw. During training the restored-domain model started at R@1 0.2533 against v3's 0.3365 and recovered to 0.3262 | matched domain to matched domain, restoration is worth -0.007, which is nothing. That is the theoretically expected answer: a denoiser is a deterministic function of its input and cannot add information, and a six-block trunk can learn whatever denoising helps it internally. The correction matters more than the result. M66 and M91 concluded "the restorer strictly hurts" from a mismatched comparison, and the mismatch is worth -0.126 to -0.137 here, symmetric in both directions -- larger than anything restoration itself does. A teammate's pair-relation model reporting 0.274 on raw against 0.378 on restored is almost certainly the same artefact mirrored, since that model was trained on restored tiles and scored on both. Restoration is a lever for the PICTURE, which is what lets the colour averaging be dropped, and not for the solver

M210 | Is there a cut-off at which the field only corrects colour instead of replacing it | NO / THE CHOICE IS BINARY | scratchpad/no_averaging.go.py, 16 boards. The team's objection to the shipped submission is the colour averaging, and it is a correct reading: at sigma 20, one fragment wide, everything coarser than a tile comes from the predicted field. Raising sigma should leave the fragments their own colour at every scale a viewer reads as colour and let the field set only the global cast, which is ordinary colour correction. Tested at alpha 1, where the picture is the assembly with its low frequencies swapped | flat fill 0.3511. No field at all 0.2601 at detail 24.9. Shipped point, alpha 0.5 sigma 20: 0.3129 at detail 12.0. At alpha 1: sigma 20 gives 0.2652, sigma 40 0.2566, sigma 60 0.2540, sigma 100 0.2525, sigma 160 0.2514 | the field is worth something only while the texture is suppressed. At full texture sigma 20 buys +0.005 and every larger cut-off is WORSE than not using the field at all, so the hoped-for middle ground does not exist. The decision is binary: the composed picture at 0.3129, or the restored assembly with no substitution anywhere at 0.2601, a difference of about 0.05 on the platform. Built both -- `submissions/composed_v1` and `submissions/honest_v1` -- so the choice is a judgement about the manual review and not about what is available

M211 | Filtering components by self-consistency before packing | NO EFFECT / THE HARVEST IS TOO FINE-GRAINED | scratchpad/filter_comps.py, 12 boards. M206's criterion used as a filter rather than as an argmax: drop the components that do not confirm themselves, then pack, swept over the threshold | keeping everything gives place 0.0087 at adjacency 0.190, and every threshold up to 0.9 keeps 99.7% or more of the components and changes nothing. Restricting the criterion to tiles with two or more in-component neighbours makes it worse, not better: of 32 components per board only 15 are testable at all and their mean consistency is 1.000 | the harvest the pipeline feeds the packer is made almost entirely of two- and three-tile components, and those are self-consistent by arithmetic: with a single in-component neighbour the test reduces to whether the fused matrix agrees with the agreement-filtered edge that built the component, and it nearly always does. M206's signal lived in components of six tiles or more, which are a minority here. The criterion is a core CHOOSER, where it works well, and not a filter. A correction to the reasoning first written into that script: one-neighbour tiles are NOT confirmed by construction -- the component edges come from the agreement filter and the test uses the fused matrix, which are different objects -- and the discrimination M206 measured came precisely from those tiles

M212 | Agreement across BOTH axes of diversity | KEY / FIRST BREAK OF THE 0.155 CAP | scratchpad/two_axis_agreement.py, 10 boards. Two mechanisms have produced gains and they diversify differently: M167 crosses INPUT (the same matcher on raw and on restored tiles), M201 crosses ARCHITECTURE (two matchers of equal strength). They had never been crossed with each other. Four scorers -- v3 and local, each on raw and on restored tiles -- each through the full calibrated path, then mutual-best edge sets intersected | singles: v3/raw 687 edges at 0.461 precision and 0.012 of the board in internally-correct components; loc/raw 687 at 0.462, 0.008; the restored views 731 at 0.335 and 735 at 0.334. Both models on raw: 543 at 0.545, clean tiles 0.025. One model, both views (the M167 mechanism): 312 at 0.678, clean tiles 0.127. Three of four: 333 at 0.674, 0.117. ALL FOUR: 256 edges at precision 0.747 and clean tiles 0.194 | 0.194 breaks the cap that four unrelated schemes landed on -- loop closure 0.154, two-model agreement 0.158, seven-model vote 0.159, unfrozen trunk 0.158 -- and which M163 recorded as an information ceiling. It is not: the ceiling was an artefact of diversifying along ONE axis. Architecture and input produce different kinds of error, and their intersection is worth far more than either alone (0.194 against 0.127 and 0.025). Note also how sensitive the harvest is to precision rather than count: both models on raw supply twice the edges at 0.545 and yield an eighth of the clean coverage, because a component is clean only if every tile in it agrees

M213 | Does the two-axis harvest reach the layout | PARTIALLY / BEST ADJACENCY THE PROJECT HAS HAD | scratchpad/two_axis_downstream.py, 10 boards, packed by the same packer with restarts swept | shipped harvest: 165 edges, 51.7 components, place 0.0104, adjacency 0.192 at restarts 1 and 0.196 at 3. Two-axis unanimity: 256 edges, 74.8 components, place 0.0064 at restarts 1, 0.0073 at 3, 0.0031 at 8, adjacency 0.211 / 0.216 / 0.216 | adjacency rises from 0.192 to 0.216, the best this project has recorded, and absolute placement FALLS from 0.0104 to 0.0064. The two are not the same quantity and never have been: M121 measured that at place_acc near zero the whole SSIM gain from assembling comes from adjacency, at about 0.135 SSIM per unit, so +0.024 of adjacency is worth roughly +0.003 before post-processing and less after it (M174). The upstream gain is real and converts into relative structure, not into absolute position, which remains the separate unsolved half

M214 | Three architectures times three inputs: the k-of-9 curve | KEY / THE CAP WAS AN ARTEFACT OF ONE AXIS | scratchpad/nine_scorers.py, 8 boards. M212's two axes taken to three each: v3, local and wide as architectures, raw plus two per-tile restorers as inputs, nine scorers each through the full calibrated path, edges kept by number of agreeing scorers | votes 4 -> 475 edges at precision 0.565 and 0.049 of the board in internally-correct components; 5 -> 380 at 0.637, 0.086; 6 -> 307 at 0.707, 0.145; 7 -> 190 at 0.821, 0.244; 8 -> 157 at 0.857, 0.254; 9 -> 128 at 0.891, 0.259 | 0.259 against a ceiling of 0.155 that M163 recorded as informational after four unrelated schemes landed on it. It was not informational: every one of those schemes diversified along a single axis. Architecture and input make different mistakes, and requiring agreement across both at once reaches a place none of them could. The old frontier is not beaten in EDGE terms -- 128 at 0.891 sits below 113 at 0.95 -- yet the clean coverage is two thirds higher, because unanimity across diverse scorers keeps edges that cluster into whole correct components rather than scattered correct pairs

M215 | Which point on the k-of-9 curve the LAYOUT wants | ACCEPTED / BEST ADJACENCY THE PROJECT HAS HAD | scratchpad/nine_downstream.py, 8 boards, each selection packed by the deployed packer with restarts 1 and 3 | the shipped harvest packs to place 0.0104 at adjacency 0.192. By votes: 5 -> place 0.0015 adjacency 0.233; 6 -> 0.0100 and 0.234; 7 -> 0.0093 and 0.183; 8 -> 0.0091 and 0.173; 9 -> 0.0080 and 0.154. Restarts change nothing at any point | six of nine is the operating point: adjacency 0.234 against the shipped 0.192, a fifth better, at the same placement accuracy and for no cost but eight extra forward passes. The shape of the curve is the lesson. Clean coverage rises monotonically with the vote threshold and the final layout's adjacency PEAKS in the middle and then falls, because a stricter filter leaves the packer fewer edges and it fills the remainder with the weak seam score M160 measured at 0.349. The best harvest is not the cleanest one; it is the cleanest one that still constrains the whole board

M216 | The voted harvest end to end, with adjacency finally instrumented | THE TRADE, MEASURED | src/harvest_votes.py wired into src/infer_composed.py as `--votes`, validated on 24 boards against the two-view filter that shipped. The validation report never printed adjacency, which is the quantity that matters when placement is near chance (M121), so it now does | six of nine votes: adjacency 0.221, place 0.0040, 73.7 components, SSIM 0.2500 at detail 24.5. The old two-view filter: adjacency 0.194, place 0.0049, 52.6 components, SSIM 0.2571 at detail 24.9 | the assembly is 14% better and the metric is 0.007 worse. That is M174's law in its sharpest form to date -- a real, measurable improvement in how much of the picture is correctly joined does not pay, because more of the board is placed as rigid blocks whose absolute position is still wrong. The two submissions therefore want different settings: the score wants the old harvest, an assembly judged by eye wants the voted one. Instrumenting adjacency in the shipping validator is the durable part: for two months this pipeline reported place_acc, which sits near chance and moves by noise, and not the number that actually describes the assembly

M217 | Absolute placement re-tested on the better harvest | STILL CLOSED / AND IT NAMES WHAT WOULD OPEN IT | scratchpad/place_components.py, 12 boards, the six-of-nine vote harvest. Every previous closure of absolute placement (M108, M161, M196) was measured on a harvest that put 0.155 of the board inside correct components; M214's reaches 0.259 with larger pieces, and a larger piece is constrained by its own bounding box, so the search might have shrunk faster than the evidence weakened | 29 internally-correct components of six or more tiles across twelve boards, median size 7 tiles in a 4x4 box. Admissible positions: median 462 of 576, tenth percentile 371, minimum 320. Placed by the colour field: 0.000. By the row prior: 0.000. Chance, one over the admissible count: 0.002 | the harvest improved and the geometry did not. A 4x4 component can sit in four hundred and sixty places, so the bounding box constrains almost nothing, and neither criterion beats one in five hundred. This is the fifth independent closure of absolute placement and the first that says what would change it: a component 20 tiles wide has five admissible columns and one 24 wide has one, so the route to placement runs through components an order of magnitude larger, not through a better way of choosing among positions for small ones

M218 | Widening the input axis to five | NO / IT IS EXHAUSTED | scratchpad/fifteen_scorers.py, 8 boards. Three architectures times five inputs -- raw plus all four per-tile restorers, including the two whose residual sigma is 40.9 and 42.2 against 34.9 delivered, since a scorer earns its place by erring differently rather than by being good | best clean coverage 0.243 at ten votes of fifteen, against 0.259 for nine scorers at nine votes; best adjacency 0.232 at six votes, against 0.234. The largest internally-correct component peaks at 13.9 tiles | more inputs do not help. The two extra restorers make the tiles worse as images and their errors are not usefully different, only noisier, so they dilute the vote exactly as M186's restored view diluted a fusion and as M208's third architecture did. Three inputs and three architectures is the plateau of both axes

M219 | A third axis, free: score every seam with the OTHER head | ACCEPTED / BEST ADJACENCY THE PROJECT HAS RECORDED | scratchpad/transpose_axis.py, 8 boards. The matcher carries four heads and they do not share weights, so transposing every tile turns a horizontal seam into a vertical one and the same model judges the same edge through different parameters. One extra forward pass, no training, and nothing in this repo had used it. Eighteen scorers: three architectures times three inputs times upright and transposed | adjacency by vote threshold: 8 -> 0.245, 9 -> 0.238, 10 -> 0.237 at place 0.0100, 11 -> 0.227, 13 -> 0.188. Clean coverage peaks at 0.262 at fifteen votes and the largest internally-correct component reaches 15.1 tiles at thirteen | adjacency 0.245 against 0.234 for the upright nine and 0.192 for the harvest that ships -- the best assembly this project has produced. At the vote threshold that matches the shipped placement, 0.0100, adjacency is 0.237. The gain over nine scorers is small and the cost is double the forward passes, so it is worth taking only where assembly quality is the goal; the axis itself is the finding, since it is the first new source of diversity since M167 and it required no training at all

M220 | Merging verified components, ranked by contact self-consistency | REJECTED | scratchpad/merge2.py, 10 boards, the six-of-nine harvest, offsets enumerated more widely than M180's grid-bounded set so the numbers are not directly comparable | 308 pairs of internally-correct components. Top-1 correct: seam score 0.026, contact self-consistency 0.010, the two combined by rank 0.010 | the criterion that picks a correct component 91.7% of the time as an argmax (M206) cannot pick a correct MERGE at all. A component confirms itself over its whole interior, which is many independent judgements; a merge is tested only along its contact, which for these pieces is two or three tiles, and that is the same short-seam regime M180 already measured at 0.147. Better components did not help because what limits the test is contact length, not component quality

M221 | Component size is BOND PERCOLATION, and that unifies the activation threshold | KEY / THE MECHANISM BEHIND THE KNEE | scratchpad/percolation.py. The 24x24 lattice has 1104 true adjacencies. Correct edges placed at random among them, largest connected component and covered share measured over 40 trials per point | 100 correct edges (fraction 0.091) -> largest 5.2, covered 0.306; 161, which is what the six-of-nine harvest supplies -> 8.0 and 0.453; 250 -> 14.0; 350 -> 27.8; 450 -> 70.0; 552, the percolation threshold at fraction 0.500 -> 272.4 and 0.929; 650 -> 508.9; 750 -> 560.1 | component size is not a property of edge precision, it is a phase transition in edge COUNT, and below the threshold no amount of cleaning produces a large piece. This explains M102's knee mechanically for the first time: the solvers switch on at edge precision 0.72 and R@1 near 0.47 because half of 1104 is 552, and R@1 0.50 is exactly the point at which the correct-edge graph percolates and components become board-sized. Two measurements taken a month apart are one phenomenon. It also corrects M166's target: 400 edges at precision 0.88 is 352 correct edges, which the simulation puts at a 28-tile largest component -- not a solvable board. The real target is about 552 correct edges. Our unfiltered mutual set already holds 342 of them at precision 0.496; filtering to 0.707 cuts it to 217. Note finally that our correct edges CLUSTER better than random -- the harvest's largest clean component measures 15.1 where random placement of the same count predicts 8.0 -- which is the one direction in which the threshold can be cheated

M222 | Concentrating the edge budget instead of spreading it | REJECTED | scratchpad/concentrate.py, 8 boards. M221 left one door open -- our correct edges cluster better than random, 15.1 tiles against a predicted 8.0 -- so the budget was spent deliberately: strict seeds, then a lower vote bar for edges that TOUCH what is already built, repeated until nothing more attaches | seed 15 grow 10 gives 283 edges at precision 0.743, clean 0.090, largest 9.0, adjacency 0.228; the flat threshold at ten votes gives 332 at 0.688, clean 0.123, largest 9.0, adjacency 0.237. Every seeded variant is at or below its flat counterpart | after two rounds nearly every tile is adjacent to something already accepted, so "touches the build" stops being a constraint and the scheme degenerates into the flat threshold with fewer edges. Concentration would need a criterion that stays selective as the build grows, and adjacency to the build is not one

M223 | More CORRECT edges does not mean bigger components -- a correction to M221 | KEY / REFINES THE TARGET | scratchpad/edge_supply.py, 8 boards. M221 put component size on a percolation curve in the number of correct edges, so the obvious move was to stop discarding candidates: mutual-best keeps about 700 of 1104 directed edges, top-1 per row keeps all 1152, and the exact Hungarian assignment keeps 1152 under the one-to-one constraint the true layout satisfies | mutual best 703 edges holding 335 correct, largest clean component 2.0, adjacency 0.191. Top-1 per row 1152 edges holding 369 correct -- MORE correct edges -- largest clean component 1.6, adjacency 0.180. Hungarian 1152 holding 349, largest 0.5. Voted six of nine: 307 edges holding only 218 correct, largest 12.4, adjacency 0.234 | the percolation curve assumes only correct edges are laid down. In a mixed set the wrong ones glue unrelated regions together, and a component counts as clean only if every tile in it agrees, so one false edge destroys a whole cluster. Contamination costs more than extra correct edges buy, which is why the supply with the FEWEST correct edges produces components six times larger. The target is therefore not 552 correct edges but 552 correct edges AT HIGH PRECISION -- about 600 edges at 0.9, against the 128 at 0.891 the strictest vote supplies now

M224 | Votes and margin swept together | KEY / PRECISION 1.000 IS REACHABLE, AND STILL TOO FEW | scratchpad/vote_margin.py, 8 boards, eighteen scorers. An edge is kept when at least k of them call it mutually best AND the weakest of those margins clears m. Only the vote axis had ever been swept | at margin 0: 6 votes 764 edges at 0.427, 8 votes 435 at 0.599 (adjacency 0.245, the best), 10 votes 332 at 0.688, 13 votes 197 at 0.816. Adding margin: 10 votes at 0.5 gives 154 edges at 0.858 with clean coverage 0.267, the highest recorded; 10 at 1.0 gives 90 at 0.948; 8 at 2.0 gives 41 at 0.997; 13 at 2.0 gives 37 edges at precision 1.000 | the margin knob reaches places the vote count cannot -- votes alone top out at 0.942 and the pair reaches 1.000, so perfectly reliable edges ARE obtainable. Thirty-seven of them per board, which percolation prices at components of seven tiles. In edge terms the new frontier is no better than M166's (90 at 0.948 against 113 at 0.95, 197 at 0.816 against 200 at 0.88); what is better is how the edges cluster, which is why clean coverage reads 0.267 against a former ceiling of 0.155. Best adjacency stays at eight votes and no margin

M225 | The harvest settings, validated on 24 boards through the shipping path | THE ASSEMBLY-VERSUS-SCORE TABLE | src/infer_composed.py with --votes and --margin, no field, alpha 1, 24 held-out boards | two-view filter (what shipped): adjacency 0.194, place 0.0049, SSIM 0.2571. Nine scorers at six votes: 0.221, 0.0040, 0.2500. Eighteen scorers at eight votes: 0.231, 0.0018, 0.2511. Eighteen at ten votes and margin 0.5: 0.164, 0.0040, 0.2551 | the best assembly and the best score are different settings and the gap is stable: eighteen scorers at eight votes give a fifth more adjacency than the filter that shipped and 0.006 less SSIM. Adjacency is now instrumented in the shipping validator, so this trade is visible at build time rather than inferred

M226 | How the leftover cells are filled is worth NOTHING | KEY / CLOSES A WHOLE CLASS OF WORK | scratchpad/fill_study.py, 12 boards, no field anywhere, so the fill supplies the colour rather than only texture. The components own about a fifth of the board and 301 cells per board are filled by rule; four rules compared -- the packer's seam score, most-constrained-cell-first by neighbour colour, the same with the seam breaking ties among the ten closest colours, and RANDOM | seam 0.2500 SSIM at adjacency 0.235, colour 0.2500 at 0.230, colour with seam 0.2498 at 0.231, random 0.2499 at 0.228. The spread across all four is one ten-thousandth | filling half the board at random is indistinguishable from the best rule we have. This follows from M70 -- SSIM has zero tolerance for displacement, so a wrong tile is a wrong tile whichever wrong tile it is -- but it had never been measured directly, and it retires every fill rule in the repository: fill_smooth, the colour assignment of M177, the seam fill the packer performs, and any future one. Only tiles placed EXACTLY right change the score, and at place_acc 0.002 there are about one of them per board. The corollary is the sharpest statement of where effort belongs: nothing between "correct" and "random" exists on this metric
