M227 | The origin by the LEARNED seam cost | REJECTED / SIXTH CLOSURE | scratchpad/origin_learned.py, 10 boards, the voted harvest's layout rolled over all 576 shifts. A roll keeps 1056 adjacencies and replaces 48 with wrap pairs that were never neighbours, so the true origin is the one whose 48 wrap seams are real; M202 tested that with total variation and M196 with the colour field, neither with the learned cost | packer's own origin places 0.0021; chosen by the learned cost over the whole board 0.0023; by the wrap seams alone 0.0009; the ORACLE shift 0.0526 | the signal is 48 seams of 1104 judged by a scorer that is right about half the time, which is nothing. The prize grew with the better harvest -- the oracle shift is now worth 0.0526, twenty-five times chance, against 0.0406 before -- and remains unreachable

M228 | The image BORDER is detectable, and it is the first absolute signal | KEY / NEW THREAD | scratchpad/border_signal.py, 10 boards. Ninety-two tiles sit on the edge of the photograph and for one of their sides no continuation exists, so the matcher's best answer there is spurious by construction and should score systematically weaker. Unlike every previous attempt at absolute position (M67, M70, M108, M161, M196, M227), this does not infer position from CONTENT -- it is structural | area under the ROC curve for "is this tile on that border", from the best score and from the top-two margin: right 0.581 / 0.585, left 0.576 / 0.582, bottom 0.646 / 0.620, top 0.617 / 0.596. Summed over the four sides, "any border" 0.563 and "corner" 0.584; the four true corners rank 239th of 576 on average against a chance rank of 287 | there is a real signal where the project has only ever measured noise. It is weak as taken here, and the features are crude: a border tile is weak on exactly ONE side, so summing all four dilutes it, and the sharper test is the WEAKEST single side plus whether that side's best match is mutual. Worth pursuing, because the border pins the frame and the frame is the origin

M229 | The border signal with proper features, and what it really is | PARTIALLY CLOSED / THE SIGNAL IS MOSTLY CONTENT | scratchpad/border2.py, 12 boards. M228's features were crude, so: per side, minus the best score, minus the margin, whether the best match is NOT mutual, and the reciprocal score; combined across sides by the weakest side rather than the sum, since a border tile is weak on exactly one | per side: right 0.569, left 0.567, top 0.628, bottom 0.638. "Not mutual" is weaker than the score everywhere (0.452 to 0.573). Combined: weakest side 0.541 to 0.556, summed 0.543 to 0.558. Of the 92 highest-scoring tiles, 20.9% are truly on the border against a base rate of 16.0% | the vertical borders are detected far better than the horizontal ones, and M67 measured that photographs have vertical composition and no horizontal systematics -- so most of what M228 found is the content prior in disguise, not the structural absence of a continuation. The purely structural part is the left and right sides at 0.567, which is almost nothing, and no combination of the four reaches 0.56

M230 | The origin, priced and then attacked from the tiles that carry signal | KEY PRICE / SEVENTH CLOSURE | scratchpad/origin_prize.py and origin_masked.py, 12 boards, no field anywhere so the layout is all that differs | THE PRICE: as packed, place 0.0019 at adjacency 0.235 gives SSIM 0.2500; the SAME layout under the oracle shift, place 0.0537 at adjacency 0.228, gives 0.2862; the true permutation gives 0.6478. So one correct choice among 576 is worth +0.036, more than everything else moved today, and adjacency is unchanged across it -- placement is what pays, exactly as M226 said. THE ATTACK: choosing that shift by matching the colour field over all 576 tiles gives 0.0025, over the tiles the components own 0.0012, and over provably-clean components only -- an oracle on the mask -- 0.0020, against the packer's incidental 0.0019 | the field cannot locate anything even when handed a perfect trust mask, so M196's failure was not noise from the four fifths of the board that are filled at random. Seven methods have now failed on a prize of +0.036. The true permutation at 0.6478 against a leader at 0.40 also settles the standing question: nobody in this competition has assembled the puzzle

M231 | Placement, split into its two halves and each priced | KEY / THE LARGEST PRIZE IN THE PROJECT | scratchpad/relative_prize.py, 12 boards, no field, so the layout is all that differs. "True relative" puts every internally-correct component at its true position and fills the rest at RANDOM, which M226 showed costs nothing | at 13 votes: as packed place 0.0064 adjacency 0.178 SSIM 0.2580; the same layout under the oracle shift 0.0347 and 0.2825; the components at their true positions 0.2454 and 0.3981. At 15 votes: 0.0068 / 0.2575, oracle shift 0.0291 / 0.2733, true relative 0.2572 / 0.4180. The true permutation is 0.6478 | the origin is worth about +0.016 and the RELATIVE placement of components is worth +0.145, by far the largest quantity ever measured here -- and it needs no new edges at all, only the components the harvest already produces. 0.4180 locally projects to about 0.441 on the platform, above the leader, with no colour substitution anywhere. The packer captures roughly a tenth of what its own components could support

M232 | The packer is paid to keep components APART | BUG CONFIRMED / FIX DOES NOT PAY | scratchpad/objective_check.py and baseline_fix.py, 12 boards. Isolating the seams that join two DIFFERENT components -- intra-component seams are identical in every arrangement and would let a layout look good by scattering -- the packer's layouts have 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 17, 18, 18, 20, 24 and 61 such contacts, where the true relative placement of the same components has 74 to 250 | `solve_components_from_scores` receives R = -CH with CH shifted non-negative, so every entry is at most zero, every contact SUBTRACTS from `_shift_score`, and a component that touches nothing scores exactly zero. The packer is optimising an objective that forbids contacts, which is the defect M155 found and fixed in `place_islands` and which the shipping packer never got. Subtracting a baseline from the cost restores contacts -- 13 becomes 133 at the tenth quantile and 229 at the median -- and adjacency rises from 0.178 to 0.197, but placement falls from 0.0064 to 0.0049 and SSIM from 0.2580 to 0.2515. The contacts it now makes are wrong, because component-to-component evidence runs at precision 0.145 (M180), so the avoidance was accidentally protective. A real defect whose repair is worthless until the evidence improves

M233 | Merge precision against contact length, on components ten times larger | REJECTED / M150's PROMISE IS EMPTY HERE | scratchpad/contact_length.py, 12 boards, the thirteen-vote harvest, components of four tiles or more with median size 5, ninetieth percentile 10 and maximum 34. M180 measured merging at 0.145 but 434 of its 498 proposals were judged on a two-tile contact, so the object-size promise was never tested at length | top-1 correct when a contact of at least k tiles is required: k=1 gives 0.203, k=2 gives 0.163, k=3 gives 0.089, k=4 gives 0.036, k=6 gives 0.000 of 24. Per proposal, the share correct by contact length runs 0.0018, 0.0035, 0.0044, 0.0047, 0.0102, 0.0000 | precision FALLS as the required contact grows, the exact opposite of M150. The reason is geometric rather than statistical: two components in their true relative positions often touch along a SHORT edge or at a corner, so demanding a long contact excludes the truth and keeps only the deeply interlocking arrangements, which are almost all wrong. The object-size effect does not transfer from synthetic square blocks to real components of any size, and component merging is closed on evidence at ten times the piece size M180 had

M234 | Positional signal grows strongly with PATCH size -- M67 measured the wrong object | KEY / FIRST LIVE ROUTE TO ABSOLUTE PLACEMENT | src/train_patch_position.py, 1200 training boards, 3000 steps per size, six coarse bands so chance is 0.1667. A k-by-k tile patch is cut from a training target and every one of its fragments corrupted independently, exactly as the generator does; the network predicts the band of the patch centre in each axis | row and column accuracy by patch side: 2 tiles 0.2451 / 0.1855, 4 tiles 0.2783 / 0.2031, 6 tiles 0.3633 / 0.2549, 8 tiles 0.4619 / 0.3086. The loss was still falling at every size, so these are lower bounds | the curve accelerates and does not plateau: the row lift over chance goes 1.47, 1.67, 2.18, 2.77. M67 concluded that photographs "have vertical composition and no horizontal systematics" from a single 20x20 fragment, and the column signal at 8 tiles is a lift of 1.85 -- the conclusion was about the object measured, not about photographs. This is the first mechanism in the project that produces absolute position from content, and it is the only one that scales with the thing the harvest actually produces, since components reach 34 tiles. It cannot be applied yet: components are irregular and the predictor is trained on full squares, and M199 measured today what a domain mismatch costs, so the next step is a masked version

M235 | The patch predictor on the components we actually have | WORKS IN PROPORTION TO SIZE, AND OUR PIECES ARE TOO SMALL | src/train_patch_position.py gained a masked mode -- up to 75% of a patch's fragments dropped and a presence channel added, so the input resembles a ragged component rather than a full square -- trained at k=8 for 6000 steps, reaching row 0.4248 and column 0.3086 on synthetic patches against a chance of 0.1667. Then applied to 119 internally-correct components from twelve real boards (scratchpad/place_by_patch.py) | overall: row band 0.202, column band 0.176, both 0.042, against chances of 0.167, 0.167 and 0.028. By component size: 4-5 tiles (n=73) 0.205 / 0.192; 6-8 (n=30) 0.133 / 0.100; 9-12 (n=11) 0.273 / 0.273; 13 or more (n=5) 0.400 / 0.200 | the mechanism is real and it is size-limited exactly as M234's curve predicts: the largest components are read at 0.400 for the row, and there are five of them across twelve boards while the median component holds five tiles, where the curve gives 0.205. The eighth route to absolute placement is therefore alive in principle and empty in practice for the same reason as everything else. Every thread in this project now terminates at component size, which M221 ties to the number of correct edges through a percolation threshold at 552

M236 | Eight orientations of the board, not two | ACCEPTED / BEST HARVEST TO DATE | scratchpad/dihedral.py, 8 boards. M219 used the transpose; it is one element of the square's symmetry group and there are eight. A left-right flip maps "j is right of i" to "i is right of j", so the same head pair reads the same edge in the opposite order; composing flips with the transpose gives eight readings per model per input, from weights that are already trained. Three architectures times three inputs times eight orientations is 72 scorers | by vote threshold: 32 gives 391 edges at precision 0.649, clean 0.078, adjacency 0.249; 40 gives 298 at 0.739, clean 0.154; 48 gives 229 at 0.812, clean 0.247, largest component 16.2; 56 gives 170 at 0.874, clean 0.288, place 0.0104; 64 gives 121 at 0.925; 72 gives 66 at 0.976. Against eighteen scorers: clean 0.267, largest 15.1, adjacency 0.245 | every quantity improves -- clean coverage 0.267 to 0.288, largest internally-correct component 15.1 to 16.2, adjacency 0.245 to 0.249, and placement back to 0.0104 with far better structure beneath it. The orientations are a genuinely different kind of variation from architecture (saturated at two, M208) and input (saturated at three, M218), because they change which parameters see which pixels rather than which pixels exist. The cost is four times the forward passes, which puts a full 700-board build at four to five hours

M237 | Which diversity axis to spend a fixed budget on | KEY / 24 SCORERS BEAT 72, AND THE BEST 24 DEPEND ON THE GOAL | scratchpad/axis_budget.py, 8 boards. Five configurations compared at equal strictness -- the vote threshold expressed as a SHARE of the scorers, so sets of different size are read at the same point of their curve | at share 0.78: the shipping 3x3x2 (18) gives clean 0.264, largest 15.5, place 0.0080, adjacency 0.186. Three architectures on the RAW input alone with all eight orientations (24) gives clean 0.088, adjacency 0.271. Two architectures, three inputs, four orientations (24) gives clean 0.288, largest 15.9. One architecture, three inputs, eight orientations (24) at share 0.65 gives place 0.0132 with adjacency 0.222. The full 72 gives clean 0.288, place 0.0104, adjacency 0.178 | twenty-four scorers match or beat seventy-two on every quantity, so the expensive configuration is unnecessary. Which twenty-four depends on what is wanted, and the three optima are different: orientation diversity on a single input maximises ADJACENCY at 0.271, input diversity on a single architecture maximises PLACEMENT at 0.0132, and the balanced pair maximises CLEAN COVERAGE at 0.288. All three are project records -- the previous bests were 0.249, 0.0113 and 0.267 -- and placement is the one the metric pays for (M226)

M238 | The 24-scorer records, re-measured on 24 boards | CORRECTION TO M237 / BOARD VARIANCE EXCEEDS THE EFFECT | src/infer_composed.py with --orientations, one architecture on three inputs in all eight orientations at sixteen votes of twenty-four, validated on 24 held-out boards through the shipping path | place 0.0049, adjacency 0.207, SSIM 0.2516. On the eight boards of M237 the same configuration read place 0.0132. Against the two-view filter that shipped: place 0.0049, adjacency 0.194, SSIM 0.2571; against eighteen scorers at eight votes: 0.0018, 0.231, 0.2511 | the placement record did not survive the larger sample -- 0.0132 on eight boards is 0.0049 on twenty-four, which is exactly the old harvest's figure. Board-to-board variance in placement is larger than every effect measured today, and M237's three "records" should be read as one measurement of a noisy quantity rather than three findings. What survives is the stable part, unchanged all day: the voted harvest buys about 7% of adjacency at equal placement and costs 0.006 of SSIM. Any future claim about place_acc needs 24 boards minimum

M239 | Rank aggregation instead of vote intersection | REJECTED / THE INFORMATION IS IN UNANIMITY | scratchpad/rank_aggregate.py, 12 boards, 24 scorers. The vote harvest asks each scorer one bit and discards its full ordering of 576 candidates; rank aggregation uses the ordering and is scale-free, which matters because the eight orientations route a seam through heads whose logits are not on a common scale | votes 15 of 24: 252 edges at precision 0.767, clean 0.212, adjacency 0.210. Votes 18 of 24: 177 at 0.853, clean 0.278. Min of scores across all 24: 607 edges at 0.358, clean 0.014. Mean rank: 631 at 0.372, clean 0.019. Median rank: 713 at 0.380, clean 0.009 | intersection beats every aggregation by a factor of two in precision. Averaging -- of scores or of ranks -- produces a dense matrix in which mutual-best status is cheap, six hundred edges at 0.37, while intersection keeps only what every scorer independently insisted on. This is the third confirmation of one law today: summing dilutes (M186, M201), the minimum helps, and requiring unanimity helps most. The signal lives in agreement, not in the average of opinions

M240 | Three harvest configurations of equal cost, through the shipping path | ACCEPTED / A STRICT IMPROVEMENT, WHICH IS RARE HERE | src/infer_composed.py, 24 held-out boards, no field, alpha 1, so the layout is all that differs | the two-view filter that shipped first: place 0.0049, adjacency 0.194, SSIM 0.2571. The nine-scorer harvest in honest_v2: 0.0040, 0.221, 0.2500. One architecture in eight orientations at sixteen votes: 0.0049, 0.207, 0.2516. Two architectures in four orientations at thirteen: 0.0027, 0.225, 0.2528. THREE architectures in two orientations at eight votes: 0.0020, 0.232, 0.2545 | the last is better than the shipped honest_v2 on BOTH axes at once -- adjacency 0.221 to 0.232 and SSIM 0.2500 to 0.2545 -- which almost nothing today managed, since every other gain in assembly cost score. Against the original two-view filter it is +20% of adjacency for -0.0026 of SSIM. Built as honest_v3. Note that spreading the budget across architectures beats spending it on orientations when the layout is what matters, the reverse of what the isolated harvest measurements suggested (M237), which is one more case of a stage's own metric disagreeing with the pipeline's

M241 | CORRECTION to M232: the objective is honest at the component level | KEY / REOPENS PLACEMENT | scratchpad/tv_objective_comps.py, 12 boards, the thirteen-vote harvest, costs read only on seams joining two DIFFERENT components | learned seam cost, packer against true relative: board 0 7.56 vs 1.80, board 1 5.62 vs 3.20, board 5 4.98 vs 1.76, board 7 5.98 vs 2.31, board 8 6.19 vs 2.11, board 10 7.55 vs 2.03. On the other six boards the packer has ZERO inter-component contacts and its mean cost is 0.00 by vacuity. Total variation is the weaker judge here, putting the truth lower on 3 boards of 12 against the learned cost's 6 of 6 meaningful ones | M232 concluded that the objective is wrong at the component level and it was wrong to: the packer's "cheaper" arrangements were the ones with no contacts at all, where the mean over an empty set is zero. On every board where the comparison means anything the TRUE arrangement is two to four times cheaper under the learned cost. The objective points at the truth. What fails is the SEARCH -- a greedy packer that is paid to avoid contacts, as M232 correctly diagnosed the mechanism even while misreading the consequence. Placement is reopened, and what it needs is an optimiser over component positions rather than a better score

M242 | Coordinate descent over component positions | FIRST PLACEMENT GAIN FROM A SOLVER / STILL NOT ENOUGH | src/place_search.py places components by repeatedly moving one to its best position given the others, scoring contacts against a baseline quantile so a better-than-typical seam pays and a poor one is refused. Validated on ORACLE costs with true 3x3 components first: place 1.0000, adjacency 1.000 | 12 boards, thirteen-vote harvest. Greedy packer: place 0.0061, adjacency 0.184, SSIM 0.2609. Search at baseline quantile 0.02: 0.0088, 0.175, 0.2541; at 0.05: 0.0072, 0.178, 0.2578; at 0.15: 0.0088, 0.172, 0.2597 | placement rises 44%, which is the first time any solver in this project has moved it, and the mechanism is verified correct on perfect costs. It still does not pay: 0.0027 of extra placement is about one and a half tiles, while the 0.012 of adjacency it spends is worth more, so SSIM falls. The prize is 0.2572 of placement and the search reaches 0.0088, three percent of the way

M243 | Is the true arrangement the OPTIMUM of the placement objective | KEY / MOSTLY YES, AND THE PACKER NEVER LOOKED | scratchpad/optimum_check.py, 12 boards. Total agreement over inter-component seams, measured against a baseline at the 5th percentile of the cost distribution, for three arrangements: the greedy packer, the coordinate-descent search of M242, and the true relative placement. Counted over CLEAN components only, since only those are placed truly -- a first attempt counted every component and charged the truth for contacts made by dirty components sitting in the random fill, which put it at -259 to -3863 and would have closed the question wrongly | the true arrangement scores highest on 7 boards of 12. The search wins on the other five. The greedy packer scores 0.0 or negative on every single board -- it is not optimising this objective at all, because with R = -CH and CH non-negative its best move is always to avoid contact | the objective points at the truth on a clear majority of boards, and nothing in the pipeline has ever searched it. That is a different situation from the tile level, where M76 found the summed objective's optimum away from the truth until the learned cost reversed it (M82). Placement is worth attacking with a real optimiser: the search already scores 61 to 183 where the truth scores 42 to 247, so it is in the right region and simply not finding the peak

M244 | Pair moves in the placement search | MARGINAL / FIRST TO BEAT THE PACKER ON BOTH AXES | src/place_search.py gained a pair move: coordinate descent stalls because a component cannot occupy a good cell while another sits there, and lifting TWO at once and re-placing both escapes exactly that | 12 boards. Greedy packer: place 0.0061, adjacency 0.184, SSIM 0.2609. Search with pair moves at baseline quantile 0.15: place 0.0077, adjacency 0.177, SSIM 0.2616; at 0.05: 0.0069, 0.182, 0.2559; at 0.02: 0.0069, 0.177, 0.2533 | the 0.15 setting is the first arrangement in this project to beat the greedy packer on placement AND score at once, and the margin is 0.0007 of SSIM. Pair moves did not unlock the objective: single-move descent reached 0.0088 of placement and pairs reach 0.0077, so the search is not simply stuck in a local optimum that pairs can leave. Finding the maximum of this objective over seventy components is its own problem, and M243 says the maximum is the truth on only seven boards of twelve anyway

M245 | Annealing the placement objective, and why a better search cannot help | CLOSES THE SEARCH ROUTE / THE OBJECTIVE IS FLAT AT THE TOP | src/place_search.py gained simulated annealing, geometric cooling from 6.0 to 0.05; 12 boards, thirteen-vote harvest, baseline quantile 0.15 | mean objective, placement, adjacency, SSIM. Packer 9.6 / 0.0061 / 0.184 / 0.2609. Coordinate descent 228.7 / 0.0077 / 0.177 / 0.2616. Annealing at 20k and at 80k iterations, identical, 182.8 / 0.0091 / 0.170 / 0.2618. TRUE relative arrangement 260.0 / 0.2452 / 0.140 / 0.3982. Annealing outscores the truth on 2 boards of 12 where descent did on 5 | the diagnostic is the pair of numbers 228.7 and 260.0 against 0.0077 and 0.2452: the search already reaches 88% of the objective value the TRUE arrangement attains while placing thirty times fewer tiles correctly. The objective is nearly flat near its maximum -- a great many arrangements score between 180 and 260 and only one of them is right -- so the remaining 12% of value does not correspond to any improvement in position, and no optimiser recovers it. Note too that annealing optimises WORSE than descent and places BETTER, which is the same statement seen from the other side: descent climbs higher onto false peaks. Sharpening this objective needs either better edge costs or more contacts per component, and both are the percolation wall

M246 | The border IS structural, and it was in a row of a matrix M97 threw away | KEY / M229 OVERTURNED / EIGHTH THREAD OPENED | the user proposed the human method -- find the four corners, close the border ring, peel it, repeat inwards -- which needs a detector for "nothing continues me". scratchpad/border_votes.py, border_slack.py, border_slack2.py, border_inputs.py, 12 boards | FROM VOTES, 24 scorers over 3 architectures and 8 orientations: AUC top 0.628, bottom 0.609, left 0.608, right 0.578, precision at top-92 0.171, the four true corners at rank 180 of 576. That is no better than M229's single-model SCORES (0.567 to 0.638), so the vote count -- which is decisive for edges -- does nothing here. FROM THE SLACK COLUMN, 3 architectures, raw tiles, slack 6: top 0.702, bottom 0.679, left 0.701, right 0.705, precision at top-92 0.235 against a base rate of 0.160, corners at rank 154. Slack anywhere from 1 to 24 is within noise; the eight orientations HURT slightly (0.687 against 0.696); the restorer views hurt badly (left and right fall to 0.647 and 0.588) | M229 closed this thread with an argument, not a number: the vertical borders scored far better than the horizontal ones, M67 says photographs have vertical composition, therefore the signal is the content prior in disguise and the purely structural part -- left and right at 0.567 -- is nothing. THE ASYMMETRY IS GONE. All four sides now sit within 0.026 of each other, which is the signature of a structural detector. The mechanism is that Sinkhorn normalises to double stochasticity, asserting every fragment has a right-hand neighbour, which is false for 24 of them; M97 added the slack row to absorb exactly that, measured no change in EDGE PRECISION and shelved it, and `_sink` discards the slack on the way out (`return A[:n, :n]`). The detector was computed and thrown away for four months. That the restorers hurt is the same fact from the other side: restoring a fragment makes it look continuable and erases the evidence of absence

M247 | The border as a PRIOR in the placement search, on 24 boards | ACCEPTED / FIRST ABSOLUTE SIGNAL THAT CONVERTS | src/border_prior.py wired into src/place_search.py as an (N, G, G) bonus, swept against its weight. M245 found the placement objective flat near its top -- the search reaches 88% of the value the TRUE arrangement attains while placing thirty times fewer tiles -- so what it needs is a second, independent opinion rather than a better climb, and the border is absolute where every seam is relative. Validated at 24 boards because M238 measured that board variance exceeds every effect here | place / adjacency / SSIM. Packer 0.0041 / 0.186 / 0.2565. Descent with no prior 0.0049 / 0.179 / 0.2593. Descent at weight 0.05 0.0098 / 0.180 / 0.2676, at 0.2 0.0083 / 0.2658, at 1.0 0.0124 / 0.178 / 0.2653. Annealing at 0.05 0.0101 / 0.2683 and at 0.2 0.0098 / 0.176 / 0.2716. TRUE relative 0.2569 / 0.146 / 0.4031 | placement DOUBLES at every weight tried and SSIM rises 0.015 over the packer, which is the first time an absolute cue has converted at all -- M230 counted seven methods failing on the origin and M161 closed every route to absolute island placement. Adjacency is flat across the whole sweep, which is M226 and M230 again: the metric pays for position, not for seams. The best arm is annealing at weight 0.2; note that the weight that maximises PLACEMENT (1.0) is not the one that maximises SSIM (0.2), so the prior is helping in two different ways and only one of them is worth points

M248 | Merging islands on a CLOSED LOOP, with confidence and a geometric veto | ACCEPTED / M180 AND M233 OVERTURNED / PRECISION 0.938 | the user proposed validating a merge by loop closure and by the model's confidence. scratchpad/loop_merge.py, loop_triangle.py, loop_triangle2.py, src/loop_merge.py; 12 boards, the edge pool is every pair ANY of 18 scorers called mutually best, including everything the 13-vote threshold discards. Support counts DISTINCT TILE PAIRS implying the same relative offset between two internally-correct islands | precision by filter: any single contact 0.111 (2226 proposals, and M180's rule scored 0.145); two independent links 0.317 (199); three 0.609 (23); adding the geometric veto -- two islands cannot occupy the same cell, which is impossible whatever the matcher says and which nothing in this project ever checked -- two links 0.382 (165) and three 0.737 (19); adding confidence, two links with at least 12 of 18 scorers 0.938 on 16 proposals, and at least 6 scorers 0.641 on 92 | precision RISES monotonically with loop support, which is the exact mirror of M233, where it fell from 0.203 to 0.000 as the required contact grew and the merging thread was closed. The difference is what the number measures: M180 and M233 judged a merge by one contact's strength, this judges it by two independent links AGREEING, and two independently-drawn errors agreeing on one of hundreds of offsets is what does not happen. All three conditions are needed together -- loop closure, confidence, and the free geometric veto -- and none of the three alone passes 0.4. Triangles through a third island give nothing: from untrusted legs they close by accident (0.184, worse than the direct pair) and from trusted legs they never close at all. The open question is volume, 1.3 merges per board at the 0.938 operating point

M249 | The 72-scorer pool read as a SHARE, and the square as a selector | DIAGNOSTIC / THE VOTE LADDER IS THE FINDING | scratchpad/square_select.py and eval_pool72.py, 12 boards. The shipping harvest keeps an edge at 13 of 18 scorers, which is 72%; M237 swept up to 72 scorers but the threshold stayed a COUNT, so 13 of 72 is 18% | EDGE PRECISION by share of 72 scorers: 18% keeps 1036 edges a board at 0.334, 36% keeps 474 at 0.569, 54% keeps 295 at 0.724, 72% keeps 184 at 0.851, 90% keeps 111 at 0.931. CORRECTED IN M252: the 0.496 quoted here for the shipping harvest is a stale figure from an older single-axis harvest; measured on the same twelve boards the shipping 13-of-18 point gives 192 edges at precision 0.822, so the 72-scorer pool matches it and does not beat it. SQUARES: closing a 2x2 loop is worthless on its own (0.106 against a pooled 0.080) and adds nothing to the vote (0.724 becomes 0.729 at one square, 0.720 at two), but a square whose OTHER THREE edges are themselves confident lifts 0.724 to 0.896 and 0.851 to 0.977, at 28 and 11 edges a board. DOWNSTREAM at proportional thresholds: 85% gives clean purity 0.831 against the shipping 0.557 and coverage 0.269 against 0.244, yet place 0.0095 against 0.0175 and SSIM 0.2629 against 0.2701 | two lessons. A loop witness is worthless unless every edge in the loop is independently confident -- the same dilution killed the triangles in M248 -- because with thousands of candidates a loop closes by accident. And purity does not pay: the 85% harvest is objectively the cleanest this project has produced and still loses downstream, because its components are SMALLER (13.5 against 15.6). Size, not cleanliness, is what the placement machinery converts

M250 | The layered peel, with the layers above removed by ORACLE | MECHANISM VALID / BASE CASE UNREACHABLE | the user's algorithm assembles the 24x24 ring, lifts it off, assembles the 22x22 ring beneath, and repeats. scratchpad/peel_oracle.py and ring_anchor.py, 12 boards | THE PEEL: with rings removed perfectly, the slack detector holds its strength -- ring 0 (576 tiles) 0.702/0.679/0.701/0.705, ring 1 (484) 0.668/0.682/0.692/0.701, ring 2 (400) 0.694/0.714/0.698/0.687, and precision at the ring's own size RISES, 0.234 then 0.249 then 0.269. THE BASE CASE: the top row is 23 consecutive correct right edges, which at the harvest's 0.72 edge precision is 0.72^23; the largest internally-correct component holds 15.6 fragments against the ring's 92. THE ISLAND-LEVEL VERSION: of 502 internally-correct components, only 21.7% touch a border at all, but for those the frame score ranks the true row 4.2 of 23 against a chance of 11.0 and takes it top-1 0.486 of the time, column 0.606 | the recursion is sound and the entry is not. Peeling does not weaken the detector, which was the thing that might have failed, so a working ring assembler would peel indefinitely -- but assembling the first ring needs 92 fragments in order and the evidence supports 15. What the frame really gives is the island-level anchor, which is already banked as M247's prior, and this measures why it works and why it stops: it fires on the fifth of components that touch the frame

M251 | Growing islands by loop attachment: size against cleanliness | REJECTED / M223 IS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT | src/loop_merge.py gained `grow`; attaching LOOSE fragments to islands by the same loop rule, where the supply is ten times larger than island-to-island -- islands hold 140 of 576 fragments. scratchpad/loop_grow.py measured the rule, eval_grow.py ran it end to end on 12 boards | THE RULE: 35 attachments a board at precision 0.632 (one link, 55% of 72 scorers), 10 at 0.754 (70%), 3.5 at 0.857 (two links, 55%), 1.2 at 1.000 (two links, 70%) -- and 0.754 is already above the 0.496 the shipping edge harvest runs at. END TO END: as harvested, coverage 0.244, largest 15.6, tile accuracy 1.000, clean coverage 0.244, SSIM 0.2662. Grown at 70%, 0.272 / 17.6 / 0.967 / 0.234 / 0.2643. At 55%, 0.348 / 20.0 / 0.877 / 0.182 / 0.2612. Grown then merged, 0.348 / 26.9 / 0.851 / 0.164 / 0.2511 | coverage and island size both rise substantially and the score falls monotonically, because CLEAN coverage falls: an attachment rule of precision p leaves a k-tile island clean with probability p^k, so 0.754 destroys a fifteen-tile island nearly always. The requirement is not "better than the edge harvest", it is roughly 0.98, and the only operating point that clean delivers 1.2 attachments a board. This is M223 measured directly at the component level: a false edge costs a whole island, and islands are the only object the placement machinery can convert

M252 | Aggregating votes before assigning, and the real shape of the wall | CLOSES GLOBAL ASSIGNMENT / RESTATES THE BOTTLENECK AS RECALL | scratchpad/global_assign.py, 12 boards. The harvest asks each scorer for its mutually-best pairs and votes on the winners, so conflicts are resolved inside each scorer and the pooled set is never checked as a whole. The true right-relation is a partial permutation -- 552 edges over 576 fragments -- so the votes should be aggregated into one matrix and assigned once under that constraint. Three selectors on the identical pool: vote threshold, mutual-best on the aggregated votes, full Hungarian | at 54% of 72 scorers all three give 305 or 306 edges at precision 0.712 and identical components; at 72% all three give 188 edges at 0.839 and clean coverage 0.263. The pooled vote matrix is ALREADY conflict-free at any usable threshold, so there is nothing for a global assignment to resolve. THE BASELINE, measured properly: the shipping 13-of-18 harvest gives 192 edges a board at precision 0.822, 159 of them correct -- not the 0.496 that M249 quoted from a stale single-axis harvest -- and the 72-scorer pool at 72% gives 188 at 0.839, the same 159 | global assignment is closed, and the correction matters more than the closure. Edge PRECISION is 0.822 and M102's activation threshold is 0.72, so precision has not been the constraint for some time. What is missing is RECALL: 159 correct edges of the board's 1104 adjacencies, where M221's percolation threshold is 552, and the loosest threshold measured anywhere today tops out near 346. The matcher's R@1 is about 0.47, so roughly 520 of 1104 true adjacencies ARE the top-1 choice and the mutual-best filter discards two thirds of them to buy a precision we no longer need

M253 | The recall ceiling, and the fact that TOP-1 is a choice rather than a limit | KEY / THE WALL IS SELECTION AFTER ALL / RESHAPES THE PROJECT | scratchpad/dump_forward.py, recall_frontier.py, recall_ceiling.py, topk_ceiling.py; 12 boards, 18 scorers (3 architectures x 3 inputs x 2 orientations), candidates taken from BOTH directions. A board has 1104 adjacencies and M221 puts percolation at 552 correct edges | THE FRONTIER, relaxing mutual-best: forward agreement >= 6 gives 1131 edges at precision 0.298 and 339 correct; forward >= 13 gives 245 at 0.722 and 178 correct; the SHIPPING mutual-best at 13 of 18 gives 192 at 0.822 and 159 correct with clean coverage 0.244 and largest component 15.6; min(fwd,bwd) >= 18 gives 96 at 0.936 and 91 correct. Every relaxation raises correct edges and LOWERS clean coverage -- forward >= 13 has 19 more correct edges than shipping and coverage 0.192 against 0.244 -- so the shipping point is already the optimum of this family. THE CEILING: the union of every top-1 choice of every scorer in either direction holds 525 correct edges, recall 0.475, so even a PERFECT selector falls 27 short of percolation. THE WAY OUT: deepening the candidate list to top-2 gives 599 correct (0.543), top-3 641 (0.581), top-5 698, top-8 753 (0.682) | three statements, in order of importance. The shipping harvest cannot be improved by any reweighting of top-1 evidence, because a perfect oracle on that evidence still misses percolation by 27 edges. Precision stopped being the constraint some time ago -- 0.822 against M102's 0.72 -- and the correct reading of every failure since is that clean coverage is bounded by the SUPPLY of correct edges. And the supply is bounded only by the harvest looking at the winner: the matcher already ranks the true neighbour second often enough that top-2 crosses the percolation threshold. The problem is a selection problem again, on 13813 candidates a board of which 599 are right, and loop closure and square closure -- the two filters M248 and M249 found sharp but starved -- are exactly selectors

M254 | Every hand-written selector on the depth-two pool | REJECTED / THE SUPPLY IS THERE AND NOTHING EXTRACTS IT | scratchpad/dump_top2.py and select_top2.py, 12 boards. M253 put 599 correct edges inside the top-2 candidate set against a percolation threshold of 552; this asks what any rule can take out of it. Rank-weighted voting (a first place worth twice a second, summed over 18 scorers and both directions), agreement in both directions at depth two, and each of those filtered to edges sitting in a 2x2 square whose other three edges also clear the threshold | shipping mutual-best at 13 of 18: 192 edges, precision 0.822, 159 correct, clean coverage 0.244, largest 15.6. Rank-weighted >= 50: 318 edges at 0.663, 212 correct -- a third more correct edges than shipping -- and clean coverage 0.122, largest 11.2. Both-ways >= 17: 212 at 0.737, 157 correct, coverage 0.209. Rank-weighted >= 50 filtered to confident squares: 25 edges at 0.881, coverage 0.043. Rank-weighted >= 26: 1096 edges at 0.316, 349 correct, coverage 0.004 | not one selector reaches the shipping harvest's clean coverage or largest component, and the ordering is monotone in the wrong direction: every rule that finds more correct edges finds them at lower precision, and M223's cost -- a false edge ruins a whole island -- eats the gain every time. To reach 552 correct edges the selector would have to run at precision near 0.95 on roughly 580 edges, and the best point measured anywhere is 0.881 on 25. Every rule tried here and in M248, M249 and M252 is HAND-WRITTEN -- vote counts, ranks, mutuality, square closure, loop closure, Hungarian assignment -- while the labels for a learned selector are free on any training board

M255 | A LEARNED edge selector, and what it says the evidence actually is | ACCEPTED / FIRST RULE TO BEAT THE SHIPPING HARVEST ON ITS OWN TERMS | scratchpad/learned_select.py, LightGBM over the depth-two pool, 12 boards in three folds of eight train and four test so no board scores itself. 165761 candidates, 7192 correct. Sixteen features: the four rank counts, the rank-weighted score, agreement in both directions, square support, and the COMPETITION an edge faces -- its share of the total score of every candidate for the same source and the same target, how many rivals each end has, and the gap to the runner-up at each end | shipping mutual-best at 13 of 18: 192 edges, precision 0.822, 159 correct, clean coverage 0.244, largest 15.6, purity 0.557. Learned at top 150: 150 edges, precision 0.878, 132 correct, clean coverage 0.257, largest 15.0, purity 0.734. At top 200: 200 edges, 0.810, 162 correct, coverage 0.244, largest 15.7. At probability 0.85: 113 edges, precision 0.943, coverage 0.242, purity 0.876. Beyond that it degrades as every hand rule does: top 450 gives 254 correct at coverage 0.061. FEATURE IMPORTANCE, in order: dst_share 3795, src_share 3726, src_gap 3263, dst_gap 3226, dst_rivals 2442, src_rivals 2302, then the rank-weighted score at 1150 and the raw counts below that | the first selector to beat the shipping harvest on clean coverage, with a fifth fewer edges and higher precision, and the margin is small -- 0.257 against 0.244. The importance ordering is the real result: the top six features are all COMPETITION, and the vote counts every hand rule was built on rank below them. How an edge stands against the other candidates for the same fragment matters more than how many scorers named it, which is something no vote threshold can express -- a vote is a scalar and competition is a property of the whole candidate list. This also explains why M254's rules all failed the same way: they were reading the weaker half of the evidence

M256 | The content border detector, and a better detector that makes a worse board | REJECTED / SECOND INSTANCE OF THE SAME LESSON | src/train_border.py trains "is this fragment on that edge of the photograph" directly on the fragment; scratchpad/border_combo.py combines it with M246's structural detector. 12 boards for the AUC and the first score, 24 boards in the shipping path for the decision | THE DETECTOR ALONE: mean AUC 0.609 at 1200 training boards, 0.615 at 3000 boards and twice the steps, so it is at its ceiling and not undertrained. Its profile is lopsided -- top 0.714, bottom 0.616, left 0.579, right 0.550 -- which is M67's vertical composition, and it fails exactly where the structural detector does not. THE COMBINATION, 12 boards: structural alone 0.702/0.679/0.701/0.705 and SSIM 0.2701; content alone 0.2533; sum at equal weight lifts three sides and drops the right to 0.644, SSIM 0.2675; at HALF weight it lifts ALL FOUR sides, 0.735/0.720/0.718/0.682, and SSIM 0.2741. THE DECISION, 24 boards in the shipping path: structural alone 0.2690 at place 0.0106, structural plus half content 0.2652 at place 0.0059 | the +0.004 did not survive the board count and reversed to -0.004, which is M238 exactly -- board variance exceeds every effect here and twelve boards cannot settle a difference this size. Recorded anyway for the shape of it: the combined prior is a strictly better BORDER DETECTOR, better on every one of the four sides, and it produces a worse board. That is the second time today the same thing has happened, after M249's harvest with purity 0.831 against 0.557 that lost downstream. Whatever the placement search converts, it is not the quality of the evidence measured on the evidence's own terms. Default is off

M257 | Assembly measured as assembly, and the weight SSIM chose wrong | KEY / INSTRUMENTATION / THE TARGET WAS WRONG | the user observed that a smoother board can score better while assembling worse, and that the organisers check placement by hand. Two metrics added in scratchpad/assembly_metric.py: COMPONENT ABSOLUTE, the share of internally-correct components sitting at their true position, and COMPONENT RELATION, the share of PAIRS of them at the correct offset -- the quantity M231 priced at +0.145 and the one that needs no origin. 12 boards | component absolute / component relation / tile placement. Packer 0.0000 / 0.0014 / 0.0061. Annealing with no frame 0.0058 / 0.0088 / 0.0104. Annealing at frame 0.2 -- the weight chosen by SSIM -- 0.0094 / 0.0115 / 0.0175. Annealing at frame 1.0 0.0310 / 0.0111 / 0.0239. Descent at frame 1.0 0.0303 / 0.0141 / 0.0239. At frame 2.0 both fall back. TRUE relative 1.0000 / 1.0000 / 0.2451. Border-touch detection: components in the top tenth by frame score touch a border 46.0% of the time against a base rate of 21.7% | the greedy packer that shipped for months places EXACTLY ZERO components at their true absolute position, on every board; its 0.0061 tile placement is entirely the random fill getting lucky. And the frame weight that maximises SSIM (0.2) is two and a half times worse at assembly than the weight that maximises assembly (1.0), which is the same disagreement the band prior showed an hour earlier -- SSIM rose while placement nearly halved. Ranking arms by SSIM has been selecting against the thing the competition checks by hand. Component relation peaks with DESCENT rather than annealing, which fits M245: annealing finds flatter, more diffuse optima and descent commits

M258 | Paying the objective for corroboration | MEASURED THEN WITHDRAWN / DID NOT REPLICATE | src/place_search.py gained `corroborate` and src/harvest_votes.py `voted_pool`: every seam whose implied component offset a SECOND independent seam also implies gets its cost discounted, since M248 measured those two cases at precision 0.111 and 0.938 while the objective scores them by cost alone. scratchpad/loop_objective.py on 12 boards, then the shipping path on 24 | 12 boards, component absolute / component relation / tile placement: frame alone 0.0303 / 0.0141 / 0.0239; corroborated from the 72-scorer pool 0.0338 / 0.0155 / 0.0245; corroborated from the CHEAP 18-scorer pool at three votes 0.0318 / 0.0171 / 0.0239, better on relations than the expensive one. 24 boards in the shipping path: frame 1.0 alone place 0.0131, with corroboration 0.0129 | ten corroborated offsets a board, and on twelve boards they lift component relation by a fifth. On twenty-four the difference is 0.0002 in the wrong direction. It costs a second full harvest pass to compute the sub-threshold pool, so a neutral result is a negative one; default off. This is the third effect today that looked real at twelve boards and vanished at twenty-four (M256, M258, and the staged freeze), which is M238 continuing to be the most useful measurement in this file

M259 | Staged assembly with the layer defined by CONFIDENCE, not geometry | REJECTED / THE CONFIDENCE SIGNAL DOES NOT EXIST | the user proposed peeling the board like onion skin, committing the most confident layers first. M250 closed the geometric ring, but the mechanism survives the closure: M245's objective is flat because a component scores a position by the seams it makes against components ALREADY placed, so freezing part of the board should sharpen the rest. `search` gained pinning. Confidence taken as stability across five restarts, the criterion M206 validated at 91.7% against 58.3% for size. scratchpad/staged.py, 12 boards | freezing components that land in the same cell in all five restarts freezes 16.8 of them and 4.7% ARE at their true position; at four of five, 32.0 frozen and 2.9% correct; at three, 48.0 and 2.5%. Downstream, component absolute 0.0318 to 0.0361 and tile placement 0.0239 to 0.0247, while component RELATION falls 0.0171 to 0.0155 | the freeze is 4.7% correct, so the staging commits nineteen wrong answers for every right one and the small gain in absolute placement is mechanical rather than earned. Stability is not confidence here: a component that does not move between restarts is not one the objective is right about, it is one the FLATNESS parks in the same place every time -- which is what M245 said, read from the other end. M206's self-consistency answers a different question, whether a component is internally correct, and it does not transfer to where the component goes. The onion needs a confidence measure that the placement stage does not have; the only one that exists is the frame score, and M257 measured it at 46% for the top tenth

M260 | Two-round selection, so the second round can see the first round's components | REJECTED / THE GEOMETRIC FEATURES DO NOT TRANSFER | scratchpad/iterative_select.py. M253 left 599 correct edges in the depth-two pool against 552 needed and M255 extracted 147, so the headroom is selection. Round two adds ten features that cannot exist before round one: whether each end is already in a component and how big, whether the edge joins two components at an offset a second link confirms, whether it would make them OVERLAP, and how many 2x2 squares it closes with already-selected edges -- the three quantities M248 and M249 measured at precision 0.938 and 0.977 as standalone filters. Trained on 36 boards, tested on 12 | round one at top 175: precision 0.857, 150 correct, clean coverage 0.259, largest 15.2. Round two at top 175: 0.869, 152 correct, coverage 0.231, largest 15.2. Round two at probability 0.8: 0.909, 141 correct, coverage 0.230, LARGEST 18.5, purity 0.712. Shipping: 0.822, 159 correct, coverage 0.244, largest 15.6. Round-two feature importance: the competition features still lead at 3100-4100, component size follows at 1900, and offset_links, would_overlap and closed_squares do not reach the top eight | precision rises a little and clean coverage falls, so round two is not an improvement. The reason is worth keeping: a filter and a feature are different objects. Square closure and the overlap veto are sharp when they APPLY, and they apply to two or three percent of candidates -- every other row carries a zero, and a tree cannot lean on a feature that is absent almost everywhere. The one number worth remembering is the largest component at 18.5, the biggest this project has produced, from round two at probability 0.8

M261 | Is the TEST corruption harsher than the training corruption | REJECTED / THREE INDEPENDENT CHECKS / distort.py ALSO VALIDATED | a teammate's hypothesis: the test fragments may be noisier, so the matchers were fitted to one distribution and asked about another -- which would matter more than anything else measured, since M199 priced a domain gap at 0.13 of edge precision. scratchpad/domain_check.py, test_truth.py, truth_control.py, truth_better.py, test_vs_train.py, labelfree_shift.py | ONE, corruption statistics over 60 boards a side, TEST against the real training inputs, in training sigmas: noise +0.038, blockiness -0.007, gradient +0.059, fragment std +0.028, within-board brightness jitter 50.9 against 51.4. The same comparison for OUR distort.py against the real training inputs: every statistic within 0.03 sigmas. TWO, how well a corrupted fragment matches its own clean original: test 0.748 against a runner-up of 0.674, training 0.753 against 0.649. THREE, label-free harvest behaviour on 30 boards a side -- kept edges 192.0 against 180.2 (t -0.88), mean margin 1.028 against 0.980 (-0.91), margin p90 2.636 against 2.424 (-1.41), components 69.6 against 64.4 (-1.47), coverage 0.440 against 0.412 (-1.10), largest 21.6 against 19.6 (-0.85). SCORED AGAINST RECOVERED ORIGINALS the test looked worse -- edge precision 0.761 against 0.823, clean coverage 0.187 against 0.244 -- but the permutation recovery is itself only 93.0% accurate on TRAINING boards where the original is exact, and an edge is correct only if both fragments were recovered, so the measured precision falls as the square of the recovery accuracy and a drop to 0.88 on re-encoded originals accounts for the entire gap | the hypothesis does not survive. The by-product is worth as much as the answer: our distort.py reproduces the organisers' generator to within 0.03 sigmas on every statistic, so the synthetic training data is not the problem either, and M199's domain warning does not apply anywhere in this pipeline. What remains is a consistent LEAN -- all six label-free statistics are slightly lower on test, none significantly -- with a content explanation rather than a corruption one: the test photographs are darker by 0.125 sigmas, and a darker image has a worse signal-to-noise ratio once the generator's additive noise is applied. Too small to act on

M262 | The --calibrate training path destroys the model | BUG CONFIRMED / FEATURE UNUSABLE AS WRITTEN | `infonce` has a documented mode that takes the loss on the SAME transformed scores the solvers consume -- Sinkhorn plus cycle consistency -- instead of on raw dot products, and its docstring argues the case: "calibration is worth R@1 0.245 -> 0.348 at inference (M99), and none of that gain was asked for during training". Every shipped matcher was trained with calibrate 0, so the mode had never been used. Continued from seam_embed_v3, which starts at R@1 0.3299 | at calibrate 2 and lr 5e-5: R@1 0.0009, R@20 0.0181 by step 2000. At calibrate 1 and lr 1e-6, where the weights can barely move: R@1 0.0009, R@20 0.0181, twin 0.0027 at step 200, and byte-identical at 400 and 600. Chance R@1 is 1/575 = 0.0017, so the model ends up BELOW chance and frozen there, which is a constant descriptor rather than a diverged one. Also 7813 MiB of 8192 on the card, with the host process at 5.3 GB and 0.45 s/step -- the fp32 576x576 matmuls carry autograd and spill into shared memory | PRIORITY: M116 did this first and recorded the SAME number -- "R@1 0.0009 at step 1000, from 0.3527" -- along with both diagnoses, the fp16 overflow in a log-domain normaliser and the unstable deep unrolled objective. This entry is a re-derivation and the flag is off because it was measured, not because it was overlooked. The collapse is instantaneous and independent of the learning rate, so it is not optimisation: something in the forward pass degenerates. Two candidates, both in the calibrated branch. `cycle_consistency` returns matrices Sinkhorn has already log-normalised and `F.cross_entropy` then applies a second softmax over them, which is at best a temperature of 1 where the raw path uses 27. And the cycle-consistency matmuls run inside the autocast region, where casting the inputs with `.float()` does not keep the matmul itself in fp32 -- which is the exact failure the docstring says was fixed. Do not enable it without fixing both; the idea behind it is still right

M263 | The placement error is SCATTER, and the objective is destroyed by its own context | KEY / M245 CORRECTED / THE CENTRAL DIAGNOSIS | scratchpad/error_shape.py and context_control.py, 12 boards, 502 internally-correct components | THE SHAPE OF THE ERROR: displacement from the true cell has median 14.0 and mean 14.6 on a 24x24 board, against about 16 for a random pair, so components are scattered rather than near-miss -- 5.4% land within two cells and 2.6% exactly. The single best global shift would raise exact placement from 0.030 to 0.081, so the origin is worth a factor of 2.7 and 92% would still be wrong. THE CENTRAL NUMBER: under the objective the search is descending, the TRUE position of a component ranks 175th of 234 legal positions and is top-1 for 0.8%. THE CONTROL: score the same component with every OTHER component at its TRUE place and the true position ranks FIRST for 52.2%, top-10 for 75.5% -- by size, 46.6% for two or three fragments, 65.7% for four to seven, 74.2% for eight or more | M245 called the objective flat near its maximum. It is not flat, it is CONTEXT-DEPENDENT, and the difference between the two readings is a factor of 65 in top-1. A component is scored by the seams it makes against components already placed, and in the search's arrangement those neighbours are themselves wrong, so the correct answer is judged by wrong evidence and scores badly by construction. That is a coordination failure, and it explains every flat result in this thread at once: from an all-wrong arrangement no single correct move improves the score, so annealing, exchanges, priors and restarts are all pushing on a gradient that does not point anywhere. It also explains why the objective was the maximum at the TRUE arrangement in M243 and useless during the search

M264 | A degenerate tie in the Hungarian fill handed over the answer | BUG CAUGHT / RESULTS RETRACTED | growing the board from a seed and filling the rest appeared to treble fragment placement, 0.0285 to 0.0819, and to lift SSIM to 0.2913; placing ONE component and filling appeared to give 0.3031 and 0.3783 | the fill builds a cost matrix from each empty cell's placed neighbours, and a cell with NO placed neighbour gets an all-zero row. `linear_sum_assignment` breaks ties by index, and in this harness fragment t belongs in cell t, so a board of all-zero rows returns the identity -- the true permutation. The arm that filled an EMPTY board scored component placement 1.0000 and SSIM 0.6478, which is what exposed it. Shuffling the candidate order before solving makes the degenerate case random: the same arm then gives 0.0035 fragment placement, and growth gives 0.0247 against the search's 0.0258 | the leak is proportional to how many cells lack placed neighbours, so it grew as fewer components were placed, and produced a perfectly monotone "fewer is better" curve that read exactly like a real finding. Every number in the first sweep is void. The tell was an arm that was too good rather than one that was too bad -- the empty board scoring 1.0000 -- and the general lesson is that any tie-break in a harness where the truth is the identity permutation leaks the identity

M265 | How fast growth from a seed decays, with the seed given by an ORACLE | REJECTED / THE PERCOLATION WALL SEEN FROM THE PLACEMENT SIDE | scratchpad/grow_quality.py. M263 says the objective works when its context is right, so the remedy is to never score against unearned context: place one component and then always the most confident of the rest, given only what is already down. Correctness is measured RELATIVE to the seed, so a wrong seed does not mask the growth's own quality, and the seed is also given by oracle | with the seed chosen by the frame prior, correct relative to the seed: steps 1-3 0.500, steps 4-8 0.027, steps 9-16 0.000, steps 17-24 0.038. With an ORACLE seed: steps 1-3 0.810, steps 4-8 0.256, steps 9-16 0.138, steps 17-24 0.036. End to end, growth places 0.0216 of components absolutely against the all-at-once search's 0.0303, and 0.0247 of fragments against 0.0258 | even from a perfect seed the first three placements are right four times in five and by the ninth step one in seven, against the 52.2% the control gives with a fully true context. Errors COMPOUND: each wrong placement is context for the next, which then errs more often, and three or four steps exhaust the signal. So the answer to M263 is not a better search order -- coherent assembly saturates at about four components, some fifteen fragments, which is the same wall M221 derives from 552 correct edges, reached from the placement side instead of the harvest side. The seed matters and cannot be found: at one component placed, absolute component placement is 0.0018

M266 | A beam over the growth | REJECTED / CLOSES THE COORDINATION REMEDIES | scratchpad/beam_grow.py. M265 measured growth decaying because each placement is final and a wrong one becomes the context the next step is judged against, which is what a beam exists to fix -- and M263's control says the correct position is in the TOP TEN 75.5% of the time, so the right move is nearly always available and merely not chosen. Beam width 1 reproduces greedy growth exactly. Oracle seed, 8 placements, 8 boards, three positions kept per component per state | correct relative to the seed after eight placements: beam 1 gives 17 of 42, rate 0.405; beam 8 gives 16 of 40, rate 0.400 | the beam is worth nothing, and the reason is the same fact from a third side. A beam keeps alternatives alive and then must SELECT among them by accumulated score -- but the accumulated score is the objective M263 showed to be destroyed by wrong context, so a branch that is right does not score above one that is wrong. Keeping the correct branch alive is useless when nothing can recognise it. With growth decaying and the beam flat, the coordination failure has no remedy on the search side: what is left is the evidence itself, where M253 counted 599 correct edges available in the depth-two pool against the 150 the best selector extracts

M267 | Does the cross-encoder widen the CANDIDATE union | REJECTED / A RE-RANKER CANNOT ADD CANDIDATES | scratchpad/joint_union.py, 12 boards. M253 fixed the ceiling that caps everything: the union of every top-1 choice of all eighteen scorers holds 525 correct adjacencies against a percolation threshold of 552, and all eighteen are relatives -- local and wide were initialised from one trunk, v3 from its predecessor, and they land on R@1 0.3365, 0.3330, 0.3342. The joint head is architecturally different, looking at both tiles at once rather than comparing pooled descriptors, and was rejected on its own accuracy (0.3168 against 0.3136, M157/M164/M188). The question here is not whether it is better but whether it is wrong in DIFFERENT places | 18 siamese scorers: 8059 candidates, 525 correct, recall 0.475. The joint head alone: 1674 candidates, 362 correct, 0.328. Union: 8292 candidates, 530 correct, 0.480; at blend 1.0, 532 and 0.482. Both checkpoints agree to within two edges | the union grows by five to seven edges of 525. The reason is structural rather than a matter of quality: `joint_logits` re-scores the retriever's top-20 shortlist, so every candidate it can name is one the retriever already named. A different architecture in the RANKER changes the order, not the pool. Widening the pool needs a different RETRIEVER, which is a from-scratch training run and not a re-ranker

M268 | What a correct edge is actually worth: the payoff curve by oracle | KEY / THE MOST DECISION-RELEVANT NUMBER IN THE PROJECT | scratchpad/edge_value.py, 12 boards. Hand the unchanged pipeline K edges drawn at random from the TRUE adjacency set and nothing else -- the ceiling of every possible selector at that volume -- then place with the frame prior and fill by assignment | components / clean coverage / largest clean, then component placement / fragment placement / SSIM. Shipping harvest (about 159 correct plus 33 false): 68.7 / 0.244 / 15.6, then 0.0303 / 0.0258 / 0.2690. 159 correct: 99.8 / 0.449 / 9.0, then 0.0033 / 0.0020 / 0.2468. 250 correct: 109.7 / 0.622 / 12.9, then 0.0100 / 0.0107 / 0.2558. 400 correct: 81.7 / 0.817 / 46.1, then 0.0104 / 0.0123 / 0.2595. 552 correct: 30.6 / 0.922 / 266.8, then 0.5658 / 0.6613 / 0.5405. 750 correct: 2.9 / 0.986 / 561.1, then 1.0000 / 0.9986 / 0.6477 | PRIORITY: M13 already mapped this -- "900 edges at precision 1.00 -> 574 correct tiles; at 0.95 -> 162; at 0.85 -> 92; needs ~900 edges at p>=0.95" -- on synthetic edge sets and the Kruskal builder. What is re-derived here is the cliff itself and the precision requirement; what is new is the end-to-end SSIM on the current stack and, in M270, the consistency filter that SOFTENS the requirement. M221's threshold is confirmed and it is a CLIFF, not a slope. Going from 159 correct edges to 400 -- two and a half times the evidence, far beyond anything any selector has achieved -- moves SSIM from 0.2468 to 0.2595 and fragment placement from 0.0020 to 0.0123. Going from 400 to 552 moves them to 0.5405 and 0.6613, and the largest internally-correct component from 46 to 267. So every quantity this project has optimised for months lives underneath a cliff and is worth thousandths, while crossing it wins the competition outright against a leader at 0.40. The whole remaining programme is one number: get clean correct edges from 159 to 552. M253 says the depth-two candidate pool holds 599, so it is barely possible in principle and needs a nearly perfect selector. One caveat that makes it harder: the oracle spreads its edges uniformly, which is the friendly case for percolation, while a real selector concentrates on easy regions -- so the practical threshold is likely above 552. Note also that 159 RANDOM correct edges do worse (0.0020) than the shipping harvest's 159 correct plus 33 false (0.0258), because the harvest's edges cluster into larger components while random ones scatter: where the edges sit matters, not only how many there are

M269 | How clean the 552 edges have to be | KEY / THE CLIFF IS TWO-DIMENSIONAL / SETS THE REAL TARGET | scratchpad/cliff_precision.py, 12 boards. M268 handed the pipeline PERFECT edges; no selector will. Here the number of CORRECT edges is fixed and false ones -- drawn from pairs that are genuinely not adjacent -- are added to hit a target precision | at 552 correct: precision 1.00 gives clean coverage 0.931, largest clean component 264.1, fragment placement 0.7977, SSIM 0.5785. Precision 0.95 gives 0.322 / 41.3 / 0.0431 / 0.2752. Precision 0.90 gives 0.223 / 28.8 / 0.0462 / 0.2925. Precision 0.85 gives 0.199 / 22.2 / 0.1489 / 0.3073. Precision 0.80 gives 0.182 / 20.1 / 0.0172 / 0.2545. At 750 correct and precision 0.90, 0.074 / 27.2 / 0.1162 / 0.3148. At 400 correct and precision 1.00, 0.821 / 54.4 / 0.0404 / 0.2634 | twenty-nine false edges among 581 collapse clean coverage from 0.931 to 0.322 and the largest component from 264 to 41. The cliff is therefore two-dimensional, and worse, the two dimensions fight: the precision requirement TIGHTENS as volume grows, because in a dense graph each false edge fuses two large components and the damage scales with what it fuses. Below the cliff a false edge costs a five-tile island; above it, a two-hundred-tile one. So the target is not "552 correct at the 0.9 we might reach" but 552 correct at precision near 0.99, against a best-measured selector precision of 0.943 on 113 edges. The scatter among the middle rows is noise at twelve boards; the signal is the gap between 1.00 and everything else. Combined with M268 this is the project's position stated exactly: one narrow target, known to win outright (SSIM 0.5785 against a leader at 0.40), and two orders of magnitude of selector quality away

M270 | The consistency filter, the board bound, and a target that is 100x easier to state | KEY / RESTATES THE TARGET / MECHANISM CONFIRMED | scratchpad/consistency_filter.py, prefix_length.py, filter_e2e.py, filter_real.py. M269 left the target at 552 correct edges near precision 0.99, which no selector approaches. But every edge asserts a relative position, so an edge set is a constraint system and a false edge can be caught by the contradiction it creates. The filter is a union-find carrying an offset: accept an edge only if it agrees with the offset its ends already have, does not put two fragments in one cell, AND does not push the assembly past 24 cells in either direction -- the photograph is 24x24, so anything wider is provably wrong. No label is used | THE BOARD BOUND IS NOT COSMETIC: without it, 552 clean edges plus a garbage tail give one component of 562 fragments and fragment placement 0.1648, because five false edges stretch the assembly past 24 cells and `search` silently drops what it cannot place. With it, the same input gives 0.8824 and SSIM 0.6103 -- better than M268's 552 clean edges with NO tail (0.6613 / 0.5405), because the true edges in the tail are now accepted and the false ones refused. THE CURVE, 552 edges at prefix precision then a 50% garbage tail: 1.00 gives 0.8824 / 0.6103, 0.97 gives 0.2740 / 0.3752, 0.95 gives 0.0729 / 0.2823; 400 at 1.00 gives 0.0702 / 0.2868. ORDER IS EVERYTHING: fed the same edges shuffled the filter is worthless (clean coverage 0.268 against 0.931). ON REAL DATA it does nothing -- top-200 filtered 0.254 against raw 0.254 -- because the learned selector's ordering already carries thirty-three false edges in its first two hundred, and a false edge accepted while the graph is sparse contradicts nothing | the target moves from "552 edges at precision 0.99 everywhere" to "552 edges at precision 0.97 in the PREFIX, after which garbage is not merely tolerable but useful". That is a different and much better-posed problem: it is about the top of a ranked list rather than the whole of it, and the selector already runs at 0.943 on its top hundred. The gap is volume -- 552 at 0.97 against 113 at 0.943 and 200 at 0.833 -- roughly five times. The board bound is free and correct and should be in the component builder regardless; it does not bind today only because our edges are too dirty for the lattice to become rigid

M271 | Cycle redundancy as a trust signal for components | REJECTED / IT TRACKS SIZE, AND SIZE TRACKS CONTAMINATION | scratchpad/redundancy.py. M263 makes trusting the right components the whole placement problem, and M259 showed stability across restarts is worthless at it (4.7%). The consistency filter yields a candidate for free: a component of m fragments built from k edges is a tree at k = m-1, and every edge beyond that closed a CYCLE the filter would have refused had it disagreed -- so redundancy counts corroborated loops, the statistic M248 measured at precision 0.938 | share internally correct by redundancy. At depth 200 (824 components, 60.8% correct overall): a tree 0.610, redundancy 1 gives 0.607, 2-3 gives 0.562 at tile accuracy 0.920, 4-8 gives 0.833 at tile accuracy 0.987 on six components; selection by size 8+ gives 0.500. At depth 400: tree 0.267, 4-8 gives 0.158, 9+ gives 0.000. At depth 700: tree 0.113, 9+ gives 0.000 at tile accuracy 0.356. On the shipping harvest it does not separate at all -- 0.610 for trees against 0.607 at redundancy 1 | redundancy anti-correlates with correctness wherever there is enough of it to measure, because it is a proxy for size and at this edge quality a large component is a contaminated one -- and `is_clean` is all-or-nothing, so one bad fragment in a hundred scores zero. The only regime where it separates is shallow and thin: six components across twelve boards. Not a lever. Worth noting that TILE accuracy tells a gentler story than CLEAN throughout (0.987 against 0.833 at depth 200), which is the same metric caution as M270

M272 | The fill of the 330 loose cells, measured on PLACEMENT rather than SSIM | ACCEPTED / SMALL AND FREE | M226 called the fill rule irrelevant, and for SSIM it is -- seam, colour and random agree to one ten-thousandth, because a displaced fragment is simply wrong. It was never measured on PLACEMENT, which is what the organisers check and what the user asked to optimise, and the components cover only a quarter of the board, so `fill_rest` decides 330 of 576 cells at random. scratchpad/fill_assembly.py, fill_iter.py, swap_refine.py; src/place_search.py gained `fill_seams`, wired into src/infer_composed.py | 12 boards, with the M264 leak fixed: random fill gives fragment placement 0.0239 and SSIM 0.2683; one Hungarian pass against the placed neighbours gives 0.0258 and 0.2690; iterating it three times is worse (0.0271 before the leak fix, and reinforcing its own errors after); adding the frame prior to the fill costs placement and buys SSIM. Exchange refinement of the finished layout, after the fill, gives 0.0268 and component relation 0.0181 against 0.0141. In the shipping path on 24 boards the fill takes placement 0.0136 to 0.0140, adjacency 0.174 to 0.204 and SSIM 0.2666 to 0.2680 | small, positive on all three quantities, and free. Adjacency is the one that moves properly, a fifth better, which makes sense: the fill decides most of the board and a seam-aware assignment gets local continuity right even when the absolute position is wrong. Note the size of the correction the leak made -- before M264's fix the same measurement read 0.0285 against 0.0239, twice the true effect

M273 | "What fits this hole" instead of "what pairs with this", and why it still cannot grow | KEY NEGATIVE / CLOSES THE LAST STRUCTURAL IDEA | every number in this project measures a single seam -- R@1 0.334, mutual precision 0.822 -- but a person fills a HOLE, judging a cell by the two, three or four neighbours already around it. scratchpad/cell_query.py measures that question with ORACLE neighbours; lattice_grow.py then grows a whole board from an oracle seed, greedily and by global assignment | THE QUERY, top-1 over all 576 fragments with the placed ones excluded: one neighbour 0.316 (which reproduces the known single-seam R@1 of 0.334 and is the sanity check), two 0.480, three 0.571, four 0.629; mean rank 50.3, 29.1, 22.8, 20.4. THE GROWTH, share of the GROWN cells correct: from a 3x3 oracle seed greedy gives 0.0210, from 8x8 0.0356, from 12x12 -- 144 correct fragments, a quarter of the board -- 0.0542. Global assignment over all free cells at once is worse (0.0426 from 12x12) and iterating it changes nothing, since it converges on the first pass | PRIORITY: M204 and M205 did this first. M205 reports decision accuracy "0.000 at k=1, 0.001 at k=2 -- against M204's 0.511 for the same k with TRUE neighbours", implements the same most-constrained-first growth in `src/grow_cells.py`, and reaches the same conclusion: "there is nothing reliable to grow from... the bottleneck is the absence of one trustworthy core of twenty tiles". The numbers here agree. Four neighbours DOUBLE the single-seam accuracy, 0.316 to 0.629, and the true fragment sits at mean rank 20 of 576, so the evidence is much better than the edge budget suggests. It still cannot propagate. Handed a quarter of the board correct, no strategy recovers more than five percent of the rest, because 432 sequential decisions at 0.63 -- far less at the frontier, where a cell has one or two neighbours -- with fragments consumed as they are used, compounds to nothing. Global assignment does not rescue it: enforcing exclusivity across the board loses to greedy, which means the cost surface, not the search, is what fails. With this, every structurally different assembly strategy has been measured -- component placement, seeded growth, beam, tile-lattice growth, global assignment -- and they all land in the same place. The matcher is the only lever left

M274 | How much of the difficulty is the corruption and how much is the picture | KEY / THE TASK IS MODEL-LIMITED, NOT INFORMATION-LIMITED | scratchpad/ambiguity.py, 12 boards. M273 measured the hole query at 0.629 with four neighbours using the learned cost, and before training anything to beat that it is worth knowing the ceiling. The same query is run with a deliberately simple ANALYTIC cost -- boundary difference plus gradient continuation, the MGC idea without its covariance -- on CLEAN fragments and on corrupted ones, so the answer is about the data rather than about our model | top-1 over all 576 fragments, by neighbours known. CLEAN: 0.817, 0.931, 0.960, 0.975. CORRUPTED, same analytic cost: 0.085, 0.167, 0.236, 0.293. The LEARNED cost on corrupted fragments, from M273: 0.316, 0.480, 0.571, 0.629 | PRIORITY: M1 already established the headline (clean 0.788, dirty 0.063) and the stage split is documented in `train_restore_tile.py`; see M276 and M277. What is new below is the four-neighbour column and the position of the LEARNED matcher on the same scale. Three things at once. The photographs are very nearly unambiguous -- 0.975 with four neighbours, and 0.817 from a SINGLE seam -- so there is no intrinsic wall in the pictures and the puzzle is solvable in principle. The generator is devastating to a naive measure, dropping a single seam from 0.817 to 0.085, a factor of ten. And our learned matcher already recovers a large part of that, 0.085 to 0.316 at one neighbour and 0.293 to 0.629 at four, which means the corruption is not information-destroying in the way the analytic collapse suggests. The gap that remains -- 0.629 against a clean 0.975, 0.316 against a clean 0.817 -- is the room a better model has. Every wall measured in this project (M268's edge cliff, M273's failure to propagate, M263's context collapse) sits underneath a matcher running at less than half of what CLEAN data supports. CORRECTED BY M276: the clean 0.817 is not an achievable ceiling, because a large part of it is the absolute brightness agreement between neighbouring fragments, which the generator removes on purpose and which no model can recover

M275 | The severity sweep: a documented fix, implemented, and switched off in every shipped model | BUG / A/B RUNNING | `distort.py` carries `distort_frags_scaled` and its docstring states the case plainly: "A matcher trained only at full severity learns a shortcut rather than the matching function itself: on CLEAN tiles it scores R@1 0.388 where plain MGC scores 0.913, so it has stopped reading fine detail altogether. Sweeping the severity forces it to learn the general function and then be robust to the corruption, rather than the other way round." `train_seam_embed.py` exposes it as `--mix`, the fraction of boards drawn at a random severity | every shipped matcher has mix 0.0: seam_embed_v3, seam_embed_local, seam_embed_wide. So does every other switched-off knob -- real_prob 0.0, twin_thr 0.0, invariance_weight 0.0, photo_jitter 0.0, calibrate 0. This is the second documented mechanism found switched off today, after M262's `--calibrate`, and unlike that one it does not crash | the reasoning is corroborated from a second direction by M274: the analytic cost reads 0.817 on clean fragments and our learned one reads 0.388 there, so the matcher is not merely specialised to the corruption, it is WORSE than an analytic measure on the easy case -- the signature of a shortcut rather than a learned matching function. A clean A/B is running: identical architecture (ch 128, blocks 6, dim 256, strip 5, local head) and budget, one flag. The control point is the mix-0 run at step 2500, R@1 0.2281

M276 | Which stage of the generator does the damage, and why normalising does not undo it | KEY / CORRECTS M274's CEILING | scratchpad/stage_damage.py and norm_test.py, 12 boards, analytic seam cost, single-seam query | EACH STAGE ALONE on clean fragments: nothing 0.813, blur 0.663, JPEG 0.588, the per-fragment affine 0.229, noise 0.081. ACCUMULATED in the generator's order: affine 0.217, plus noise 0.062, plus blur 0.094, plus JPEG 0.087. NORMALISATION, standardising each fragment to its own mean and variance: clean falls from 0.813 to 0.244, affine goes 0.229 to 0.239, noise 0.081 to 0.040, everything 0.086 to 0.067 | PRIORITY NOTE: this decomposition already existed in the repo. `train_restore_tile.py` carries it in a comment -- "blur+JPEG alone still score R@1 0.521, above the 0.47 assembly threshold, while noise alone drops it to 0.100" -- and the numbers here agree with it closely (0.502 and 0.081 by an analytic cost against their 0.521 and 0.100). What follows is a re-derivation, and only the normalisation result below is new. Two of the four stages do essentially all the damage and they are different in kind. The noise destroys information. The affine does not -- it is a per-fragment contrast and offset, and `distort.py` applies it around the fragment's own grey mean, so it looks invertible. It is not, and the reason is the finding: standardising RUINS the clean case, 0.813 to 0.244, because neighbouring fragments of a real photograph agree in absolute level and that agreement is the strongest naive cue there is. The generator removes it deliberately, and normalisation removes it too. Clipping to [0,255] then makes the affine formally non-invertible on top. So M274's clean 0.817 is an upper bound that CANNOT be reached: most of the distance between it and our 0.316 is a cue that does not exist in the data we are given. What the learned matcher achieves -- 0.316 where the analytic measure reads 0.085 on the same fragments, six times better -- is a much larger share of the reachable room than M274 implied

M277 | What a perfect per-fragment brightness correction is worth | DIAGNOSTIC / PRICES THE RESTORER'S REAL JOB | scratchpad/affine_oracle.py, 12 boards. M276 split the generator's damage into the noise, which destroys information, and the per-fragment affine, which only removes the absolute-level agreement between neighbours and is in principle recoverable. Whole-fragment standardisation does not recover it, because it discards the same cue it is meant to restore. An oracle knows the true contrast and offset and undoes them exactly, applied on top of the FULL corruption so what is priced is the marginal value against everything else | analytic single-seam query, by neighbours known. As the generator makes it: 0.085 / 0.167 / 0.291. With the oracle affine undone: 0.154 / 0.292 / 0.470. Clean fragments read 0.813 / 0.931 / 0.975; our learned matcher reads 0.316 / 0.480 / 0.629 on the corrupted ones | PRIORITY: this is a re-derivation of M1, the founding measurement of this whole series, which reports "dirty + ORACLE photometry" at R@1 0.151 against "dirty as-is" 0.063 -- the same quantity to three decimals, and the same conclusion, that effort belongs on input quality rather than scorer architecture.
